FOREWORD

Thanks

이 책이 나오기까지 기획에서부터 믿고 지원을 아끼지 않았던 미주 한국일보와
양룡 교수님, 인천방송 김원국 의원님 그리고 밤늦게까지 함께 일한 최홍석 사징님과

프리렉 직원 모두에게 감사 드린다. 아울러 미국에서 만난 많은 교수님께도 감사의 마음을 전한다.

WHY THIS BOOK
WAS WRITTEN
이 책의 초판이 나왔다는 광고를 보시고 책을 구입하고자 전화까지 주시면서 예약을 하고, 먼 장거
리도 마다하지 않고 미주 한국일보를 방문했던 많은 한인 학부모님들을 만나
면서, 유독 자녀가 작문에서 좋은 점수를 받지 못해 걱정하는 분들이 얼마나
많은지 새삼 느낄 수 있었다.

영어를 배우는 일이 한국 학생들에게 하나의 필수 과정이 된지 꽤 오래다.
그러나 유학생들이나 영어권 현지에서 자란 한인 학생들에게 가장 강한
부분이 수학이고 가장 부족한 부분이 영어이며 특히 그 중에서도 작문
이라는 것은 누구나 알고 있는 얘기가 되고 있다. 그 WritingOI 자금
미국 대학의 시작이면서 끝이다 라고 말할 정도로 중요한 관건이 되
고 있다. 세계화가 되면서 비단 대학뿐만 아니라 무역을 할 때도, 전
화보다는 메일을 통해 대부분의 무역이 이루어지기 때문에 Writing
의 중요성은 갈수록 더 커지고 있다.

그러나 대부분의 학생들은 영작문 자체를 어렵게 생각하거나 귀
찮게 생각하기 때문에 학생들이 가져야 할 가장 중요하면서도 기본적인 실력
으로 영작문으로 꼽는 영어권 대학의 방침에 따라가지 못하는 학생들이 늘고 있다. 이를 입증이라
도 하듯이 전세계의 수많은 인터넷 사이트 중 에세이나 리포트를 베낄 수 있고 또, 글을 대신 써 주
는 사이트가 대성황을 이루고 있다. 하지만, 이 인터넷 사이트가 오히려 교수들에게 학생들의 글이
베껴 쓴 것인자를 판단해 주는 방법이 되고 있어서 학생과 교수들 간에 쫓고 쫓기는 전쟁이 일어나
고 있다. 그러나 이제는 WritingOI 피해갈 수 없는 일이 되었다. 최근 TOEFL 시험에 WRTT1NG 이
필수로 바뀐 것과 마찬가지로, 미국 대학 위원회에서 발표한 NEW SAT 시험에서 WRITINGOI 필수
가 된 것은 영어가 모국어인 학생들에게조차 부담스런 일로 이를 준비해야 하는 한인 학생들에겐
긴장과 두려움으로 다가간다. 다시 말해 제대로 에세이를 쓸 수 없다면 대학의 문에 들어올 수 없
다는 강경한 대학 측의 입장을 느낄 수 있다.

이 책은 취업, 유학 또는 대학원 진학을 위해 TOEFL 을 준비하는 학생들, 미국 대학 진학을 위해

SAT 를 준비하는 학생, 메일이나 서류 작성을 많이 하는 직장인까지 영작문을 공부하고 있고、잘하
기를 원하는 한국인을 위해 준비했다. 어렵지도 그렇다고 너무 쉽지도 않은 이 책은 세 명의 미국
현직 교수가 집필한 한국인을 위한 영작문 책이다. 한국인의 에세이를 읽고 연구 분석해서 에세이
쓰는 방법을 한국과 관련된 예제를 바탕으로 설명한다. 그리고 미국 대학에서 배우는 여러 종류의
에세이뿐만 아니라 TOEFL 에세이와 같이 시험 대비를 위한 에세이 작성 방법까지 제시하고 있다.

4 Writinglecture.com

===== PAGE 5 =====
| Writing이 지금 미국 대학의 시작이면서 끝이다 라고 말할 정도로 중요한 관건이 되고 있다.
세계화가 되면서 비단 대학뿐만아니라무역을 할때도, 전화보다는 메일을 통해 대부분의
무역이 이루어지기 때문에 Writing의 중요성은갈수록 더 커지고 있다

특히, 현재 TOEFL 채점관으로서 답을 외우듯이 쓴 것으로 보이는 수많은 아시안 학생들의 TOEFL
에세이에 대해, ETS 에서 새로이 표명하는 채점 가준을 근거로 외워서 쓰는 글이 앞으로는 좋은 점
수를 절대 받을 수 없다는 중요한 메시자를 전하고 있다.

이런 책의 내용은 저자들의 인터넷 강의 (www.writingleture.com) 로도 청취할 수 있는데, 미국 현직
교수들의 강의는 더욱이 유학을 준비하고 있는 학생들에게 대학 강의에 대한 맛을 제대로 전달해
줄 것이다. 그리고 본문에 대한 자세한 설명과 연습 문제 풀이는 혼자서 하기 힘든 영작문 공부에
대해 어둠 속을 비추고 있는 밝은 등대와 같은 역할을 해 줄 것이라 믿는다

여기에 더해진 한가지 중요한 점은, 책에서 배운 이론을 바탕으로 직접 글을 써봐도 그 글이 제대
로 된 글인지 누군가 봐 주지 않으면 계속 틀린 글을 쓸 수밖에 없다. 이러한 답답한 경험을 누구나
한번쯤 가져 보았을 것이라 생각했기에, 한국인의 영작문 공부를 도와 주길 미국 전역 영문과 교수
들에게 요청하면서 돌아다니길 수개월. 미국 내 명문대학으로 꼽는 하버드, 예일 등 14 개 주의 20
여 개의 대학을 방문했고 참 많은 어려움 뒤에 이제 여러분에게 도움을 줄 촤고의 교수님들을 인
터넷 강의실에 모셔 올 수 있게 되었다. 여러 가지 주제로 쓴 글을 인터넷으로 제출하면 그 글이 맞
는지 틀리는지 그리고 어떻게 써야 하는지 교수 방까지 찾아 가지 않아도 미국 교수들의 명쾌한 답
을 들을 수 있다. 힘주어 말하지만 미국 교수를 개인 교수로 만나는 일은 전세계 어디서도 경험하
기 힘든 일이다. 이런 기회를 잘 이용하여 자속적으로 연습을 한다면 수년간 이루지 못했던 진정한
영작문 실력 향상의 맛을 느낄 수 있을 것이라 확신한다.

단어와 문법에 얽매여 에세이 쓰는 방법을 몰랐던, 다시 말해 더 큰 것을 보지 못해서 영작문에 대
해 두려움만 가졌던 여러분에게 이 책을 만난 일이 큰 기쁨이 되기를 기원하며 계속해서 더 좋은
책을 기획 구상하는 Freelec USA가 될 것을 다짐해 본다.

FOREWORD

Thanks

We appreciate The Kor은a Times and Professor Young Yang for their
support. We'd 죠Iso like to say thank you to Hong Sug Choi and all
the professors in America who have joined our team. -------------------------------- -------------------------------------—

WHY THIS BOOK
WAS WRITTEN
For students in Korea, studying English has been essential for a very long
time. Many Korean people dedicate a large portion of their lives to mastering
English. As a founding member of Freelec who took part in
publishing this book, I too have spent many years studying
English. But I still struggle with doubts about whether I'm
using English correctly.

People joke that a Korean person living in Los Angeles
could learn more about Korean than he could in Korea.
Living in Los Angeles, many Koreans simply give up
using English since they don't speak it well. Instead
they live and do business using only Korean. I also
became used to living that way. But while working
for an American company, I felt ashamed of my
English skills because I had difficulty
communicating with co-workers. I realized my English
level was that of a child, since I was forced to rely on my limited vocabulary
for communication. My frustration and desire to learn English grew with each
passing day. I bought English books and even went back to college to
improve my English. My initial goal was to speak English fluently, but I
quickly realized I needed to learn something else first. I needed the ability to
express myself more logically. I realized I wasn't going to learn how to
generate my own speech by simply memorizing phrases out of a textbook. So
I changed the way I studied. I started to write my thoughts down on paper and
read them to myself. This helped me learn to think in English and express
myself more and more freely. This practice helped my English become more
clear and specific. I started to feel more confident about my ability to learn
English. As a result, I was able to earn high grades on my reports and
presentations in the university courses. Now when I speak English, I find
myself visually writing my thoughts down in my head.

6 Writinglecture.com

===== PAGE 7 =====
With the College Board's announcement that an essay would be added to the
new SAT, even American students are feeling pressured to improve their
writing skills. Without a doubt, Korean students and their parents are getting
stressed about this issue as well. Writing in one's first language is hard
enough; writing in a second language is even harder.

This reminds me one of an article I read about a parent who discovered
something disturbing at her child's elementary school. She witnessed her son
sitting alone, coloring a piece of paper while the rest of the class was learning
to write. The teacher had to separate him from the other students and let him
do whatever he wanted so that he wouldn't become noisy and disturb other
students. The reason he was so disruptive was that he hated writing, so
instead of paying attention to what he wasn't good at, he chose to annoy other
students during writing class.

Nowadays it's not only this child who hates writing. There are many Internet
writing sites that have expanded their business. Since many students are not
proficient writers or because they don't enjoy writing, they buy their essays
and reports online from these companies, instead of doing their writing
themselves. As a result, Internet sites designed to recognize these essays sold
on the Internet are also popular with teachers. In short, there is now a war
between students and schools, with one side cheating and the other trying to
catch them.

We can't just hide and ignore this situation. It is wiser to face it and find a
solution. This book is written for the people who want to face their
difficulties with English writing and overcome them. This book is not too
difficult to understand, but at the same time it's not going to be easy either.
Presently

a

team

of

American

university

English

professors

has

been

assembled to read Korean students' essays and analyze the
professors can clearly teach how to write various types of academic essays.
And they also can teach you how to prepare for essay exams such as the
TOEFL essay and the new SAT essay.

The

students who have learned to memorize an essay instead of writing their own
original essay. The author gives specific examples of how the graders
recognize these "canned" essays. Within the next several years, policies will
change and anyone caught submitting a "canned" essay will receive a lower
grade and eventually will fail the exam altogether. It is important that
students learn to write well in their own voice when they take these tests.

To help students, we are supplementing the textbook with additional on-line
lectures on writing English essays at www.writinglecture.com. For several
months, our team traveled all over the United States to assemble a unique
team of English professors. We are now ready to help you step by step on
your journey to improve your English writing skills. Study along with the
authors' lectures and practice writing your own thoughts by yourself. We
know that your writing skills cannot improve without someone checking or
evaluating your work. You get the opportunity to receive online tutoring from
an American professor in the comforts of your home. Write your essay, and
then the American professors will help you revise and edit it. With this type
of practice you will feel confident about your writing. Do not dread writing
any longer, but discover the freedom and satisfaction of being able to write
what you intended to say with the help of this book. That is why we wrote
this book. Freelec will continue to publish many more English books tailored
help the Korean student. The book, along with the online lectures, will shine a
light on the sometimes dark and difficult path of English writing.

Spring 2004
Freelec English Dept.

토플 채점관과 마국 교수들이 말하는

PERFECT WRITING

2] 책의 전체 구성
이 책은 총 10 장으로 구성되어 았습니다. 1 장부터 4 장까지는 토플 에세이와 대학에서 쓰는 에세이의 개요를 비롯한 주의점
과 효과적인 시험 대비책을 알려주고, 어세이 종류별로 좋은 에세이에 대한 정의와 작성 방법을 알려 줍니다.

5 장에서는 문법을 바탕으로 짧은 단 문장에서 시작해 문장을 연결하는 방법, 그리고 그 문장들을 연결해 문단으로 발전시키
는 방법을 설명합니다. 이런 내용은 한국 학생들이 배워 온 문법과는 다른, 외우는 문법이 아닌 이해하는 문법으로 정리하였
습니다. 6 장과 7 장은 작문의 개념부터 미국 식으로 접근 할 수 있는 논리적인 작문 작성 방법을 보여 주는데, 다양한 한국
문화와 한국인을 예제를 들고 있어서 더욱 쉽게 접근할 수 있도록 했습니다. 특히 이런 내용들은 비단 영작문뿐만 아니라
국어 작문에까지 작문에 대해 올바르게 이해 할 수 있도록 설명하고 있습니다. 8 장과 9 장에서는 여러 유형의 실제 학생들의
샘플 에세이를 바탕으로 교정과 수정의 기술, 그리고 평점을 받는 가준 등을 보여줍니다. 아는 미국 작문 수업에서 자주 활
용하는 작문 공부의 또 다른 효과적인 방법입니다. 10 장은 자주 헷갈리는 단어들을 비교 정리했으며, 문장에서 어떻게 이를
작용하는지 예제를 보여 줌으로써 제대로 단어를 활용할 수 있도록 했습니다.

이 강의들은 작문실력 뿐만아니라 듣기 실력을 향상 시키는 좋은 기회가 될 것입니다.

각 장의 연습 문제는 보고 듣는 작문 수업이 아니라 직접 해보는 작문 수업이라 생각하고 꼭 시도해 보십시오.
그것만이 진정으로 작문실력을 향상 시킬 수 있는 유일한 방법입니다. 연습 문제의 정답은 인터넷으로 교수들

PERFECT WRITING

Yo니r thesis has two parts:

1. Main claim Support the use of uniforms in school
2. Main reason For the safety of students

The body of your essay presents several main points that tell why your thesis is tr니e:
1. Promotes equality of students
2. Improves bonding between students
3. Reduces gang violence
4. Increases standardized test scores

Only one of these points, number 3, supports the main reason, safety; it says that uniforms reduce
gang violence, which implies that students are safer with uniforms. However, there are no
explanations, examples, or facts to back this up. So the main reason in your thesis is not supported.
In the next draft of your essay you m니st solve this problem. There are two ways to do this.

Support your statement that school uniforms
make students safer. Give explanations,
examples and facts to persuade the reader
that this is true.

Change your main reason. For example,
your new thesis might be :
"I support the use of uniforms in school
because they make students eq니al."

This thesis statement seems to work better with the body of your essay since you stress equality in
your writing. However, for the next draft, you would need to explain and give more support to
your point about equality. For example, you might write something like this:

"In my neighborhood there was a student named Cho Choong-Hoon. His family was
poor so his dothes were often raggedy. Rich kids who wore designer 이othes made fun
of Choong-Hoon. He felt badly and had low status in our neighborhood. However, at
school, Choong-Hoon wore the same uniform as the rich kids. They could not look
down on him because of his clothes. So at school, Choong-Hoon had the same status as
all other students. He was j니dged on his intelligence and abilities, not on his 이othes.
Choong-Hoon became a pop니lar st니dent at our school because he was smart and
good-hearted; his clothes did not hold him back. This shows how school uniforms
benefit students by making all students equal."

how school uniforms benefit st니dents. In your next draft, I suggest you write a section

in schools

Here you are attempting to 니se statistics to support your point. That's good. However, you must

Here are some specific notes about your essay

"Students are less appealed to what other students are wearing"
This is an incorrect use of the word "appealed." I believe what you are trying to say is:

"But like most private schools there are the st니dents of the wealthy and students of

2

lower social status, uniforms tend to cover the differences between both worlds and
keep peers at a same level."

last article is incorrect: the last phrase should read, "...keep peers at the same level."

"...students at my school could roam with a feel of eq니ality...With the use of 니niforms
however comes a feel of domestication..."
Both of these phrases use the word "feel" incorrectly. Change "feel of eq니ality" to "feeling of
eq니ality" and change "feel of domestication” to "feeling of dom은stication."
Also, I don't know what you mean here by "domestication.” Domestication normally means
feeling comfortable, the way you feel when you are at home, b니t this seems to contradict your
idea in this section. In your n은xt draft, darify what you mean by the word "domestication."

"It can take away the 니niquen은ss of students and make an uncomfortable atmosphere."
It is not 이ear why wearing a uniform would make an uncomfortable atmosphere. Explain this

"Sometimes the clothing is uncomfortable to wear around others, and takes away focus

BIOGRAPHY
My name is Joel Monkarsh. I will be assisting you with your English
language studies. I can provide Korean students with practical help as they
master English, because I have taught English for many years, including six
years in Korea. Since I spent several years in Korea and I have a Korean
spouse, I understand the many difficulties Koreans face in their attempts to
master my mother tong니e.
I lived in South Korea from mid-1994 to early 2000. In the beginning, I
experienced a great deal of culture shock, because Korea is really very
different from Europe and America. I taught English at the Pagoda Foreign
Lang니age Academy and at Soong Sil University. By remaining in Kor은a for
several years, I was able to see the good and the bad sides of Korean culture,
education, business, and politics.
Although I am no longer living in Korea, I am still ask은d by many Koreans to
convert Konglish papers into English, including cover letters, university papers, all kinds of applications,
research papers, government forms, and legal documents.
I have a B.A. degree in Political Science fr이n the University of Toledo, a M.B.A. degree in Marketing from
Seton Hall University, a legal assistant certificate from Merritt College, a TESOL certificate
from Transworld Teachers, and a Linguistics Diploma from the University of Surrey.
In 2000, my wife and I relocated to the San Francisco Bay Area. We live in San Leandro, a town of about
90,000 people, which is less than 30 minutes by subway from San Francisco. Now, I teach English in the
Alameda public schools.
My association with Freelec allows me to assist Koreans to become more fluent in my mother tongue
and to gain insights into American culture. Freelec has the added benefit of allowing Korean students to
gain the maximum fl니ency without the expense and emotional stress of having to live in the United
States. I am sure our relationship will be m니tually satisfying.

===== PAGE 41 =====
몇년전 중급 작문 수업에서 늘 하던 것처럼 글 쓰기에 대한 충고를 한 적이 있었다. ESL 수업을 수강하는 중이라도
앞으로 취업할 직장에 대비해서, 언어 구사력을 최대한 개발하라고 강조했었다. 수업 시간 후에 이 충고에 귀 기울인 한 학생
이 찾아와 선생님께서 말씀하신 것이 자기 현재 상황에 꼭 맞아 떨어지는 것 같다고 얘기를 하면서, 언어 능력이 부족해 직장
을 못 잡고 있어서 다시 2 년제 ESL 교실로 돌아왔다고 했다. 그 학생은 명문 대학에서 수학을 전공하고 그 보다 우수한 대학
의 생물 통계 분야 석사 학위를 획득한 상태였다.

엄밀히 얘기하자면 명문 대핵에서 석사 학위를 받을 정도였으니 그 학생의 학교 생활은 성공적이었다고 할 수 있다. 그런데
필수였을 작문 실력이 부족한 상태에서 어떻게 오랜 몇 년간의 학교 생활을 버틸 수 있었는지 물어 보았다. 그는 여러 친구들
의 정보들을 통해 어떤 과목을 수강할지 정했다고 했다. 역사, 철학, 영어 과목을 수강하였는데, 여기 쓴 에세이들을 과목들마
다 재활용하였으며 C 학점 이상은 받지 못했다고 했다. 그리고 운 좋게 대학에서 시행한 작문 시험도 간신히 통과할 수 있었
다고 했다. 그러나 자신이 선택한 다른 과목들에서는 훌륭한 성적을 얻었고, 특히 수핵에 뛰어나서 GRE 수학 시험에서 좋은
성적을 받았기 때문에 대학원에 진학할 수 있었는데 대학원에 진학한 후에도 똑같은 방법을 통해 석사 학위를 얻을 수 있었
다고 했다.

그런데 그런 학생이 왜 ESL 수업을 듣고 있는 걸까? 그는 직장을 잡을 수 없었다. 직장 생활에서 필수 조건인 영어로 언어
소통하는 것을 너무도 오랫동안 무시하고 살았기 때문이었다. 대학 생활을 하면서 쓰기를 필요로 하는 과목을 피했을 뿐만 아
니라 원어민과의 교류도 의식적으로 피하면서 그가 만났던 사람들은 대부분 같은 언어를 쓰는 동료나 친구들뿐이었다. 따라

Correctly Use Quotation Marks

Whenever you use another writer's exact words, you must put them in quotation
marks. Even if you've only borrowed a few words, you must acknowledge that you didn't come
니p with those words on your own: You are 니sing the carefully chosen words of someone else.

Consider this example:

더라도 자신이 직접 생각한 글이 아니라는 것을 알려 줘야 하며, 인용한 글들은 조심스럽게 사용한다. 다음 예를

Most teachers will want you to introduce your quotations. It can be disconcerting to read an
essay and r•니n into a quotation without warning. The phrases we use to introduce quotations are
sometimes called Signal phrases" because they signal that a quotation is about to begin. The
following is a list of common signal phrases:

H 대부분의 교수들은 인용문을 사용할 때 미리 알려 주도록 가르친다. 에세이를 읽는 도중에 사전 예고 없이
갑자기 인용문이 나타나면 당황스럽기 때문이다. 인용문이 곧 나타날 것이라고 알려 주는 관용구를 보통 "signal

phrase"라고 한다. 자주 사용되는 signal phrase에는 다음과 같은 것들이 있다.

According to,
In the words of,
As explains,
claims that

Notice that in the correct example above, the quotation is introduced by the phrase "In Gatto's
words."

H 앞에 나온 정확하게 인용 부호를 사용한 에세이에서는 인용 부호 앞에 "In Gatto's words，”라는 표현을 사용
하였다.

There is an important exception to the rule requiring you to use quotation marks. If you quote

more than four lines, you should not use quotation marks. Instead, block indent your
quotation, that is, set it apart from the rest of your text and begin every line five

spaces from your left margin. Here's an example of a quotation that has been set off with a
block indent:

H 인용 부호를 사용하는데 있어서 중요한 예외 규칙이 있다. 즉, 4줄 이상을 인용할 때에는 인용 부호를
사용하지 않는다. 대신 인용문 전체를 5칸씩 들여쓰기 한다. 다음은 블록 전체를 들여쓰기 함으로써 인용
문임을 나타내는 예이다.

In his essay "Why Schools Don't Educate," John Taylor Gatto discusses some of the

We live in a time of great social cri외s. O니r children rank at the bottom of
nineteen industrial nations in reading, writing, and arithmetic. The world's

In order to avoid plagiarism, you must also document the source of your quotation. In other
words, you will need to provide information about the article or book in which you fo니nd any
q니otation in yo니r essay. There are a number of different styles for documenting sources, the
most common being the MLA (Modern Language Association) Style, which is wid이y used in
humanities co니rses, and the APA (American Psychological Association) Style, which is used in
the social sciences. Always make sure you know how your teacher wants yo니 to document your
sources.

H 표절을 피하기 위해 인용문의 출처를 적어 주어야 한다. 다시 말해 어떤 기사나 책에서 인용해 왔는지에 대
한 정보를 제공해 주어야 한다. 출처를 밝히는 방법에는 여러 가지가 있는데, MLA(Modern Language

Association) 방식과 APA(American Psychological Association) 방식이 가장 일반적이다. MLA 방식은 인문
학 분야에서 널리 사용되며, APA 방식은 사회 과학 분야에서 많이 사용된다. 교수가 어떤 방식을 원하는지 항상
확인해 두어야 한다.

Some students become so nervous about plagiarism that they even enclose paraphrases within
quotation marks. But this is also wrong. Only use quotation marks when yo니 have used the exact
words of your so니rce.

H 어떤 학생들은 표절에 너무 민감하게 반응해서 바꾸어 쓴 문장에 대해서도 인용 부호를 사용하기도 한다. 그
러나 이것은 틀린 것이다. 인용해 온 원문에 대해서만 인용 부호를 사용해야 한다.

Although it's important to learn to use quotations correctly, you should be caref니I not to use too
many quotations. Your teachers will want your voice, not the voice of your sources, to

predominate in your papers. More often than not, when you're using another writer's ideas,
you should paraphrase them, that is, put them into yo니r own words. Paraphra이ng is an
important skill, one that every college student m니st learn. But when you're paraphrasing, make
sure that you're really paraphrasing. Some students make the mistake of copying the original
writer's words and then replacing a few of them with synonyms. This is still con이dered
plagiarism. In fact, even when yo니 borrow the original writer's sentence stnjcture, you have
plagiarized.

Q 인용 부호를 제대로 사용하는 것을 배우는 것도 중요하지만 그렇다고 해서 인용 부호를 너무 많이 사용해서
도 안된다. 교수가 원하는 것은 인용문으로 생각을 표현한 것이 아닌 학생 자신의 생각을 뚜렷하게 나
타내는 것이다. 학생 자신의 생각이 인용문보다 글에 더 많이 있도록 해야 한다. 다른 저자의 생각을 차용해 올
때는 바꾸어 쓰기를 해야 한다. 다시 말해서, 차용해 온 것을 자신의 단어로 다시 풀어서 쓰는 것이다. 이런 바꾸어
쓰기는 모든 대학생들이 반드시 배워야 하는 것 중에 하나인 중요한 기술이다. 그러나 바꾸어 쓰기는 제대로 해야
한다. 어떤 학생들은 원문을 그대로 베낀 후 단어 몇 개만 동의어로 바꾸기도 한다. 이 또한 표절이다. 실제로는,
원문의 문장 구조를 차용하는 것도 표절이다.

Give Credit to Your Sources

As was noted earlier, when you use a quotation, you must cite your source. But yo니 must also
cite your source anytime you use ideas or information that you didn't come up with on your
own. If you use a statistic to support your thesis, make s니re you let your reader know where yo니
got the statistic. Don't pretend that the statistic is a result of survey you cond니cted on yo니r own.
Your teacher isn't going to believe you, and there's nothing wrong with using statistic you found
while reading -as long as you cite yo니r so니rce.

mind. Though you may not be able to tell the difference between professional writing and your
writing, your teacher most certainly can. There is a strong chance you will be caught, and if you
are, yen』might find yourself in big tro니이e. An essay you write yourself, even a poor one,

is likely to get a higher grade than a plagiarized essay. Even more importantly,
your writing will never improve if you plagiarize. So do your own work, learn from your
mistakes, and allow yourself to gradually become a better writer.

An Overview of the TOEFL Test of Written English (TWE)

The Test of Written English (TWE) is one of the four parts of the Test of English as a Foreign
Lang니age (TOEFL), which is used to eval니ate the English skills of people whose native language
is not English. The Test of Written English measures the test taker's ability to write on an
aligned topic in English. The essays are eval니ated according to a 이x-point scale that considers
the writer's ability to generate and organize ideas, to s니pport those ideas with examples or
evidence, and to respond to an assigned topic in standard written English. C니rrently the TOEFL
is taken on computers at the test sites throughout Korea.

H WE는, TOEFL을 구성하는 네 가지 파트 중의 하나로, 영어가 모국어가 아닌 사람들을 대상으로 하는 영어
능력 평가이다. TWE의 목적은 주어진 토픽에 대한 수험자의 영작문 능력을 테스트하는 것이다. 에세이는 6점 만
점을 기준으로 평가되는데, 아이디어를 생각해 내고 구성하는 능력, 적절한 예와 증거를 통해 이러한 아이디어들을
뒷받침해 주는 능력, 주어진 토픽에 대한 답변을 표준 문어체 영어로 구성하는 능력 등을 테스트한다. 현재 한국에
서는 토플이 CBT 방식으로 치러지고 있다

Test takers have 30 minutes and cannot use a dictionary. In fact, nothing can be taken into the
test room; everything needed is provided at the testing site. Scratch paper is provided for
organization and notes, but it is collected at the end of the test.

시험장에 가져갈 수 없다. 정리와 메모를 위한 연습 용지가 제공되지만, 시험이 끝나면 모두 회수된다.

하기 때문이다. 논제를 작성했으면 논제를 뒷받침해 줄 세부 항목들에 대해 간략히 정리를 해 두고, 각
세부 항목들은 반드시 논제 부분에서 제시된 의견을 뒷받침해 줘야 한다는 사실을 꼭 기억해 두기 바
란다.

After you have planned your essay, yo니 have about twenty minutes to write. Remember that
you need to write eno니gh to develop the topic and demonstrate yo니r proficiency. Academic
essays often follow a three to five paragraph model, with an introd니ction, one to three body
paragraphs, and a conclusion. The actual number of paragraphs is not as important as the
quality of the writing. Make s니re that you include a topic sentence for each body paragraph and
that the information relates back to the opinion expressed in your thesis statement. The final
paragraph should con이ude your essay by either restating or summarizing your ideas.

E 여기까지 에세이 전반에 대한 계획을 마쳤으면 약 20분 정도가 남았을 것이다. 에세이는, 토픽을 전개하고
본인의 실력을 보여주기에 충분한 정도로 작성해야 한다. 격식을 중요시하는 에세이의 경우에는 3~5개의 문단으
로 구성된다. 첫 번째 문단은 도입 부분이고, 중간의 1~3개 문단은 본문이며, 마지막 문단에서 결론을 맺는다. 그
러나 문단의 개수는 그리 중요한 것이 아니다. 중요한 것은 글의 질이다. 본문의 경우 각 문단마다 토픽 문장이 들
어가야 하며, 본문에서 제시되는 사항들은 논제 부분에서 제시한 의견과 연결되어야 한다. 결론 부분에서는 수험자
의 생각을 다시 서술하거나 요약함으로써 에세이를 결론지어야 한다.

Finally, take your last five minutes to reread your essay and edit your work. The first thing you
want to do is check your contents and make s니re that you have focused on the topic. Do yo니r
body paragraphs support the thesis? Are there enough details to illustrate your points? Is there a
concluding paragraph?

H 마지막 5분 동안에는 작성한 에세이를 다시 읽어본 뒤 교정할 부분이 있으면 교정을 한다. 우선 해야 할 일
은 내용을 체크한 뒤 토픽에 포커스가 맞춰져 있는지 확인하는 것이다. 즉, 본문이 논제를 뒷받침해 주고 있는지,
핵심을 설명하는데 필요한 세부 항목들을 충분히 서술했는지, 결론이 제대로 지어져 있는지 등을 확인해야 한다.

After yo니 look at your contents, go back and look at the language and mechanics. If yen」know
that yo니 have some weak spots such as verb tense consistency or missing articles, check for
mistakes in those areas. Is your writing 이ear and concise? Is it punctuated carefully? Make s니re
there aren't any fragments or run-on sentences since they can be confusing to your readers.

How the Essays are Evaluated

As stated previou외y, for the Test of Written English, or TWE, the essays are evaluated according
to a set of criteria determined by language testing specialists and researchers employed by
Educational Testing Service (ETS). Readers who have received extensive training from ETS do the
actual scoring. These readers are typically English, ESLZ and composition teachers from high
schools, colleges and universities across the United States. They assign each essay a score on a
six-point scale, with 6 being the highest possible score and 0 the lowest. Great care is 니sed to
make sure that the readers adhere to the scoring guide, and throughout the scoring process, the
readers are monitored carefully to make sure that they are evaluating according to the
standards set forth by ETS and TOEFL

H 앞에서 언급했듯이, 7WE 에세이는 프의 언어 시험 전문가나 연구원들이 정한 기준들에 따라 채점된다. 실
제 채점을 담당하는 채점관들은 日S로부터 엄격한 훈련을 받는다. 이들은 주로 미국 전역의 고등학교, 단과대학,
대학 등에서 영어나 ESL, 작문을 가르치는 선생님들이다. 채점관들은 6점 만점을 기준으로 0에서 6까지 점수를
매긴다. 채점관들이 채점 기준에 따라 점수를 매기도록 세심한 주의가 기울여지며 채점 과정 전반에 걸쳐 채점관
들이 ETS와 토플에서 정한 기준에 따라 채점을 하고 있는지도 세심하게 모니터된다.

The TWE essays are evaluated holistically; this means that they are looked at as a "whole" - the

essay is considered in its entirety, based on the overall impression it makes on the
reader. The same scale is 니sed to evaluate all writers, regar에ess of level. This is different from
the way essays are typically evaluated in the English classroom, where the teachers respond to
students' writing based on the I은vel of the co니rse.

Q TWE 에세이는 ‘전체적’으로 채점된다. 즉, 글을 부분별로 쪼개서 채점하는 것이 아니라, 글이 채점자에게
주는 전체적인 인상을 기반으로 채점을 한다. 똑같은 채점 기준이, 수준에 상관없이, 모든 수험자에게 동일하
게 적용된다. 이것은 코스 수준에 따라 채점 기준이 달라지는 학교 영어 수업 시간의 일반적인 채점 기준과는 다
르다.

When the TWE essays are eval니atedz they are not marked at all. The readers are trained to read
each essay once and evaluate it based on their first impression according to the criteria. Each
essay is read by at least two readers. After the second reading, the essays are checked to make
sure that the readers are in agreement as to the essay's score. If the scores are within one point
of each other, for example, a 5/6 or 2/3, then the essay is not read again; however, if there is a
difference of more than 2 points between the scores, a 4/6 or 3/5, for example, then the paper is
given to a third reader who will aid in determining the final score. The readers take the entire
process very seriou외y as it is well understood that the essays are of great importance for the test
takers.

H TWE 에세이를 채점할 때는 하나하나 꼼꼼히 따져가며 하지 않는다, 채점관들은 각각의 에세이들을 한번씩
읽으면서 처음에 받았던 인상을 바탕으로 채점 기준에 따라 채점하도록 훈련을 받는다. 각각의 에세이들은 최소한
두 명의 채점관이 읽는다. 두 번째 채점관이 읽은 후에는 각 채점관이 매긴 점수가 합당한지에 대해서 체크를 한
다. 5와 6, 2와 3처럼 각 점수들의 차이가 1 포인트 이내인 경우에는 에세이를 다시 읽지 않는다. 그러나 4와 6, 3
과 5처럼 차이가 2 이상인 경우에는 세 번째 채점관이 읽은 후 협의하여 최종 점수를 매긴다. 수험생들에게 있어
에세이가 매우 중요하다는 것을 모두 잘 알고 있기 때문에 각 채점관은 전체 채점 과정을 매우 신중하게 진행한다.

The following sample essays illustrate the types of writing that range from 6 to 0. The criteria
used by TWE readers are listed before the essays, and a brief evaluation of the essays follows
each one.

A typical essay at this level:

■ effectively addresses the writing task;

■ demonstrates syntactic variety and appropriate word choice, though it may have
occasional errors.

What A/ofto do When Writing the TWE Essay: The Canned Essay

As indicated above, the TWE readers receive extensive training in order to evahjate the essays
according to the TOEFL criteria. They are trained to evaluate what has been written rather than
what could have been written if the writer had had more time, for example. They are trained to
focus on the essay in its entirety instead of focu외ng on one particular aspect. The readers

look for essays that address the topic and develop it 이early and effectively.
H 앞에서 언급했듯이, TWE 채점관은 TWE 채점 기준에 맞게 에세이를 채점하기 위해 엄격한 훈련을 받는다.
이들은 어떤 가능성 예를 들어, 시간이 좀더 있었으면 이런 내용을 쓸 수 있을 텐데 보다는 현재 작성된 것을 채점
하도록 훈련 받는다. 그리고 어떤 특정 부분에 초점을 맞추기 보다는 에세이를 전체적으로 검토하도록 훈련 받는
다. 채점관들이 바라는 에세이는 주어진 토픽을 잘 처리해서 명확하고 효율적으로 전개한 에세이이다.
채점관들은 에세이가 주어진 토픽을 충실히 반영하고 있는지, 명확하고 효율적으로 설명하고 있는지
를 살핀다.

Furthermore, the readers look for the writer s "voice" in the essay. As you have seen
in the sam이e topics and the sample essays, the writer is always instructed to provide specific
reasons and examples or details to support his opinion. These reasons and examples develop
the individ니al writer's perspective and reveal his "voice." The readers want to know what the
writer thinks about the topic and expect him to explain himself as he develops the essay.
While there are always going to similar responses to a given topic, especially if the writers are
from the same country, the writer's own individuality should always shine through.

B

더 나아가서, 채점관들은 에세이에 작성자의 ‘견해’가 들어 있기를 바란다. 샘플 토픽과 샘플 에세

이에서 보았듯이, 작성자는 자신의 견해를 뒷받침해 줄 명확한 근거나 상세한 사례를 제시해야 한다. 이러한 근거
들이나 상세 설명들은 작성자의 통찰력과 견해를 드러낸다. 채점관들은 주어진 토픽에 대한 작성자의 생각을 알고
싶어하며, 에세이를 전개해 가면서 이를 설명해 주길 바란다. 사실 주어진 토픽에 대한 반응은 대개 비슷하기 마련
인데（특히 같은 나라에서 온 수험자들이라면 더욱 그러하다）, 작성자만의 고유한 개성이 묻어 나와야 한다.

개인적인에세이
Personal Essays

In general terms, students begin their essay writing experience by writing personal essays narrative and descriptive compositions. These essays usually focus on specific, concrete topics
and ask students to tell stories or describe people, places, or things. The instructors us니ally
customize the topics in order to elicit essays that require the students to use structures being
studied in the class.

Q 일반적으로 학생들이 처음 접하게 되는 에세이 작문은 개인적인 에세이이다. 이것은 이야기체로 어떤 사실
을 묘사하는 작문이다. 이런 에세이들은 보통 구체적이고 특정적인 토픽을 다루며, 학생들은 이야기를 전달하거나
사람, 장소, 물건 등에 대해 서술해야 한다. 보통의 경우, 학생들이 수업 시간에 배웠던 내용을 에세이에서 사용할
수 있도록 하기 위해 교수는 토픽을 적절히 조정한다.

An Overview of College Writing

For example, in a course in which students are working on the simple past tense, they might be
asked to write a narrative essay that uses the simple past tense: "Tell about a time when you
were surprised by something someone did for you." Likewise, in a course in which students are
working on prepo이tions of location, they might be asked to write a descriptive essay, which
would provide a perfect opportunity for them to use prepositional phrases: "Describe your
favorite room in your home." A lesson on adjectives might lead to a paragraph about a special
person. These essays are often only one paragraph in length as much focus is placed on the
structure of the single paragraph composition in most lower-level writing courses.

H 예를 들어, 간단한 과거 시제에 대해 배운 경우에는 다음과 같이 간단한 과거 시제를 사용하여 이야기체（나레
이티브）로 작성하도록 시킨다: “다른 사람이나 물건 때문에 깜짝 놀랐던 경험이 있었으면 그에 대해 얘기해 보시
오.” 마찬가지로 위치에 관한 전치사를 배운 경우에는 다음과 같이 전치사구를 충분히 사용할 수 있는 기술체 에
세이를 작성하도록 한다: “집에서 가장 좋아하는 방에 대해 서술하시오.” 형용사를 배웠다면 어떤 특별한 사람에
대한 작문을 하도록 유도한다. 이러한 에세이들은 대부분 한 문단으로 구성되는데, 작문 과정에서 가장 낮은 단계
인 한 문단 구조의 작문에 좀더 초점을 맞추기 위해서이다.

Complex Essays
才

As students become more advanced in their command of the written language, they begin to
write longer more structurally complex essays. The narrative and descriptive essays normally
are replaced by comparison/contrast,

이assification,

and causal analysis essays. The expectation

is that as the st니dents become more proficient in their use of the language, they are prepared to
develop topics that can be more abstract than those that are developed in narrative and
descriptive essays.

H 작문 실력이 조금씩 나아짐에 따라 학생들은 문단 구조가 좀더 긴 복합형 에세이를 작성하게 된다. 이야기체,
기술체의 에세이 대신 비교/대조, 분류, 인과 관계 분석 같은 에세이를 다루게 된다. 학생들의 언어 구사력이 향상
됨에 따라, 이야기체나 기술체의 에세이보다 더 추상적인 주제에 대해서도 글을 전개하기에 충분할 정도로 준비가
되어 있다.

Often upper-intermediate to advanced level writing assignments are based on readings and
disc니ssions from the coursework, and little if any time at all is actually spent on the language
structures. Teachers assume that the language skills have been developed and refined at the
lower levels and that the upper level students should focus on becoming better writers.

H 중상급이나 고급 과정의 경우에는 작문 과제를 수업 진도 도중에 있었던 독서나 토론에서 가져오는 경우가
많으며, 어법에 대해서는 거의 다루지 않는다. 어법에 대해서는 초급 과정에서 충분히 익혔다고 가정하기 때문이
다. 상급 과정에서는 글을 좀더 잘 쓰는데 초점을 맞추고 있다.

Whereas writing assignments in lower level courses are often evaluated as first drafts, in upper
levels, students often have more time and freq니ently submit multiple drafts of essays. This
allows the student writers to recognize that writing is a process, something with which most
experienced writers will agree. This change in approach, from the single paragraph, single draft
essay to the multiple paragraph, multiple draft essay, becomes more possible as the essays
become more complex in topic.

《^Summarizing
Summarizing is a related skill. When you paraphrase, you restate the author's ideas in your own
words, and when you summarize, you also rephrase the ideas but in an abbreviated manner.
While a paraphrase is more or less the same length as the original passage, a summary is shorter.
We use the same techniques to summarize that we use to paraphrase: varied sentence structure,
change of voice, use of synonyms and antonyms; however, we also express the information
much more concisely. Also, as with paraphrasing, it is very important to avoid copying the
original material.

원저자의 생각을 쓰지만 압축된 형태로 재구성한다. 바꿔서 쓰는 경우는 내용의 길이가 원문과 거의 비슷한데 비
해, 요약은 짧아진다. 요약을 할 때 사용하는 기법은 바꾸어 쓰기를 하는 기법과 같다. 즉, 문장 구조를 만들어 보

Watch an English-language TV show. As you watch, take brief notes about what is

write a quick summary of what you just watched. Don’t worry about your language;
pay attention to what you recall and get that on paper. Then after you have written
your summary of the show’s content, ask yourself what you thought of the show, how
you felt while you were watching itz what you learned about the language or culture.

Deborah always feels bad because she isn’t a good cook. She asks her mother-inlaw Marie to help her by giving her the recipe to one of Raymond’s favorite dishes.
Marie says she will help and lets Deborah have the recipe. Deborah follows it
perfectly, but when they eat it, it is terrible. Deborah feels worse than ever and like a
failure. She doesn’t trust Marie, so she goes to her house late at night and looks for the
recipe. She discovers that Marie didn’t change the ingredients in the recipe, but she
changed the labels on a spice jars so that Deborah’s meal would taste terrible.
I am always surprised by how badly Marie treats Deborah. She is not a very nice
mother-in-law. She is always criticizing Deborah’s qualities as a wife and mother. I
think Marie is a very selfish and insecure woman. She is very immature. I wish that
someone like Raymond would finally tell her to behave and treat Deborah with more
consideration.

4 Book reports and other types of literary analysis

Book reports and other types of literary analysis are often a component of English courses.
Teachers often assign readings and have lower-level students write reports about the contents,
while advanced st니dents analyze them. The p니rpose of these reports and analyses often
depends on the instructors and their intentions.

Q 독후갈의나 문학분석서는 대개의 경우 영어 수업에 포함되어 있다. 교수들이 읽을 거리를 할당해 주면, 고급
과정의 학생들은 그것을 분석하는 반면, 초급 과정의 학생들은 그것의 내용에 대해 보고서를 작성한다. 이런 독후
감이나 분석서들의 목적은 교수들의 의도에 따라 다르다.

In lower-level co니rses, teachers often want to make sure the students have understood the
reading assignment, so asking them to report on the contents is a good way to check. There
may be specific questions about the content that guide the st니dents. For example, st니dents may
be asked to name three ways the main character learns(7 valuable lesson from his experience. They
may need to complete an outline that chronologically works through the story.

H 초급 과정의 경우에는 과제물을 제대로 이해했는지 확인하는 것이 주 목적이므로 내용에 대한 보고서를 작성
하도록 하는 것이 좋은 체크 방법이다. 학생들을 지도하기 위해 내용에 대한 특정 질문을 할 수도 있다.（예를 들어
“주인공이 그의 경험으로부터 중요한 교훈을 배운 3가지 방법을 모두 적으시오/） 학생들은 아마도 글 전체를 시간
순서에 따라 읽으면서 아웃라인을 잡아야 할 것이다.

More advanced st니dents are often req니ired to do much more work with the literary aspects of
the material. The assignments can range from character analysis, for example, explain why the
main character behaved as he did when confronted by the police, to struct니ral analysis, for
example, why did the author develop the story(75 he did, providing information crucial to
understanding the main character's motivation only in the final chapter? (You will read more abo니t
literary analysis in Chapter 7.)

Q 좀더 고급 과정의 학생이라면 제시된 읽을 거리를 문학적인 측면에서 다루도록 할 것이다. 주어진 과제는 등
장인물 분석（예를 들어 “경찰과 맞닥트렸을 때 주인공은 왜 그런 행동을 했는가?”）에서부터 구조 분석（예를 들어
“주인공의 동기를 이해하는데 결정적인 단서를 작가는 왜 마지막 장에서만 제시하고 있는가?”）에까지 다양하다.
（이런 문학적 분석에 대해서는 7장에서 배우게 될 것이다.）

CHAPTER 10

An Overview of College Writing

There are too many types of writing assignments to list here. The important thing for the
student writer to remember is that he needs to be ready for any type of writing assignment in all
of his classes. As I explained in Chapter 1, the current trend in academia is to indude a writing
component in all courses. It would be highly imprudent for a student to think that because he is
majoring in mathematics or the sciences that he will never have to write.

Q 이렇게 여기에 언급한 것 외에도 너무나 다양한 방식의 작문 과제가 있다. 글을 쓰는 학생들이 기억해야 할
가장 중요한 것은 수업 시간에 가지게 되는 모든 종류의 작문 과제에 대해 대처할 준비가 필요하다는 것이다. 1장
에 설명한 것과 같이 최근의 학문적인 경향은 모든 수업에 작문 과제가 포함하는 것이다. 수학이나 과학을 전공하
기 때문에 작문할 일이 절대 없을 것이란 생각은 아주 경솔한 판단이라는 것을 느끼게 될 것이다.

How to Write Clear Sentences

important

that

the

of

clear,

coherent sentences. I like to remind my composition students that no one can hope to write a
good multiple-paragraph essay if he can't write a good single-paragraph essay. The same holds
true for sentence-level writing. How can anyone write a clear paragraph if he has not mastered
the basics of sentence structure?

H 예비 작가들에게 있어 명확하고 조리 있는 문장의 중요성을 인식하는 일은 아주 중요하다. 나는 학생들에게
한 문단으로 된 글도 제대로 쓰지 못하는 사람이 여러 문단으로 된 에세이를 쓴다는 것은 불가능하다는 것을 강조
하곤 한다. 어떻게 기본적인 문장 구조도 마스터하지 못한 사람이 명확한 문단을 작성할 수 있겠는가.

It never hurts to review the fundamentals of word order and sentence str니cture, especially when
the writer comes from a lang니age backgro니nd very different from English. English is a subjectfocused language, which means that normal word order dictates that the s니bject be in the
primary position. The s니bject is in turn followed by the verb, which if it is transitive, is followed
by an object. For this reason, English is referred to as an S+V+O lang니age. If it were that simple,
everyone would be happy. All you'd need to do would be p니t your words in that order and
you'd have yo니r sentences. However, the reality is that the S+V+O formula is the point of
departure for a much more complicated （ and interesting） voyage.

H 어순과 문장 구조의 기본에 대해 재검토하는 것은 결코 해가 되지 않는다. 특히 영어와 전혀 다른 언어권에
서 온 학생들에게 있어서는 더욱 그러하다. 영어는 주어가 중심이 되는 언어이다. 즉, 일반적인 단어 순서의 경우
주어가 가장 첫 번째에 위치하며, 그 다음에 동사가 오고, 타동사인 경우에는 그 뒤에 목적어가 위치한다. 이 때문
에 영어를 S+V+O 언어라고 하기도 한다. 모든 영어 구조가 이렇게 간단하다면 얼마나 좋겠는가. 단순히 단어들
을 이 순서대로 짜맞추기만 하면 문장이 될 테니. 그러나 S+V+O 구조는 좀더 복잡하고 （재미있는） 구조로의 출발
점일 뿐이다.

We can begin our look at sentences with a quick review of the three main sentence types:
simple, compound, and complex.

H 우선 3가지 기본적인 문장 형태（단문, 중문, 복문）부터 살펴보도록 하자.

Simple Sentences
A Simple Sentence is one subject-verb combination. It expresses a complete thought and is
often referred to as an independent clause, which means that it can work alone.

H 단문은 하나의 주어와 동사가 결합된 것이다. 완결된 의미를 전달하며, 단독으로 존재하기 때문에 ‘주절’이라
고한다.

Example A:
I study. = A basic S+V sentence.
I study English. = A basic S+V+O sentence

But do we stop here? Of course not. We often want or need to communicate far more to our
readers, which means we need to add time expressions, prepo외tional phrases, and so forth.

B 그런데 이게 끝일까? 물론 아니다. 독자들에게 더 많은 내용을 전달해야 할 필요가 있을 수도 있으므로 시간
에 관한 표현이나 전치사구 등을 추가하기도 한다.

Example B:
(Every night) 1 study.
(Every night) I study English.
(Every night) i study (after dinner).
(Every night) I study English (after dinner).
(Every night) I study (in my room) (after dinner).
(Every night) I study English (in my room) (after dinner).

How은ver, we can do a lot more with a simple sentence:

Q 이 외에도 단문에 대해 할 수 있는 것들은 매우 많다.
We can use the coordinating conjunctions and, or, and but. And expresses the idea of addition;

or is used for choices or negatives, and but is used for contrast.

H 우선 and나 or, but 같은 등위 접속사(等位 1=4)를 사용할 수 있다. and는 추가를 의미하며,(기는 선택이
나 부정을, but은 대조를 나타내기 위해 사용한다.

Compound Sentences
Compound Sentences are formed by two or more pairs of subject-verb (:이nbinations or two
or more simple sentences (or independent clauses). These sentences are often very similar to
simple sentences with compound subjects or verbs; however, the difference is the presence of a
subject before each verb:

H 중문（重文）은 두 개 이상의 주어-동사 조합이나 두 개 이상의 단문（또는 주절）으로 구성된다. 이러한 문장은
단문에서 복합 주어나 복합 동사를 사용한 것과 유사한 형태를 가지기도 한다. 차이점이 있다면 각 동사마다 주어
를 가진다는 것이다.

Example:
Every evening i study English, and I listen to mu이c.

We form compound sentences by using the coordinating conjunctions that we use for simple
sentences: and, but, and or. But we also have a few others: so, for, yet, and nor.

B 단문에서 사용했던 등위 접속人Kand, or, b니0를 사용해서 중문을 구성할 수도 있지만, so나 for, yet, nor 등
을 사용할 수도 있다.

As you can see, to add information, use the coordinating conjunction and:

H 의미를 추가하려면 등위 접속사 and를 사용한다.
Example A:
Every evening 1 study English, and i listen to music.

To express a purpose, use for:

To express a result, use so:

Complex Sentences

Complex Sentences are the next step up the ladder of sentence-level sophistication. Students
typically learn these patterns after they have worked on simple and compound sentence
patterns because they involve, as the name implies, a more complex stmcture.

H 봎Bl幻은 문장을 더 세련되게 만드는 다음 단계이다. 일반적으로 학생들은 단문과 중문을 공부한 뒤 복문
에 접어 들게 되는데 이는 이름에서 알 수 있듯이 복문은 더 복잡한 구조를 띄기 때문이다

While simple and compound sentences are formed with independent clauses, complex sentences
are formed by a combination of independent and dependent clauses.

체로서는 직관적으로 의미를 이해하기가 좀 힘들기 때문에 종속 접속사를 붙여서 주절과 연결한다.

Step j 주절
I studied for my final exams. = independent clause

I got very little 외eep. = independent clause

In the examples above, we have independent clauses (S + V). They express complete thoughts
and can work alone. However, if we add subordinating conj니nctions, they become dependent
clauses:

B 위에 있는 두 문장은 모두 주절(S + V)이다. 그 자체로서 완결된 의미를 전달하며 독립적으로 존재할 수 있
다. 그러나 이들 앞에 종속 접속사를 덧붙이면 다음과 같이 종속절이 된다.

Step 之 종솦절
After I studied for my final exams = dependent clause

Beca니se I got very little 외eep = dependent clause

Notice that these are dependent because alone they do not express a complete idea. We need
more information in order to know what happened after the speaker st니died for final exams or
the consequence of the speaker's getting little

이eep.

We complete these ideas by adding an

independent clause:

H 이들은 그 자신만으로는 어떤 완결된 의미를 전달하지 못하므로 종속적이라고 표현한다. 작성자가 기말 시험
준비를 한 뒤에 어떤 일이 발생했는지, 잠을 거의 자지 못한 결과 어떻게 되었는지를 알기 위해서는 더 많은 정보
가 필요하다. 위 문장에 다음과 같이 주절을 붙여주면 의미가 완성된다.

Step 3 종속절과 주절의 결합 예제 1
After I studied for my final exams, I was exhausted.

Because I got very little 외eep, I did not do well on the exams.

Writers like to use complex sentences because of their flexibility. The dependent clause can
come at the beginning of the sentence, or it can follow the independent clause:

H 복문은 융통성이 매우 뛰어나기 때문에 많이 사용된다. 종속절은 문장 시작 부분에 올 수도 있고, 주절 뒤에
올 수도 있다.

Step 4 주절과 종속절의 결합 예제 2
I was exha니sted after I studied for my final exams.

I did not do well on the exams because I got very little 외eep.

About the only thing we need to remember is that if we put the dependent
이ause

at the beginning of the sentence, we need to set it off with a comma.

As you can see in the sentences above, the comma is

니nnecessary

if the

independent clause comes first.

B 한 가지 기억해 두어야 할 점은 종속절이 문장의 첫 부분에 위치하는 경우에는 종속절의 끝에 콤마를 찍
어줘야 한다. 위의 문장에서처럼 주절이 맨 처음에 오는 경우에는 콤마를 사용할 필요가 없다.

One

of

the

biggest

problems

writers

run

into

when

writing

complex

sentences

is

incorrect punctuation. I tell my students that they face this problem because they have
learned their lessons well. They have learned to punctuate 이mple sentences, S 十 V (+0),
and when they see this combination, they automatically come to a full stop. The problem
is that they put a period where they should be putting a comma. Incorrect punctuation of
complex sentences is one of the main causes of sentence fragments, a serious error. It is
serious because it essentially tells the readers that the writer doesn't know where the
complete thoughts begin and end, not something a writer wants to tell his readers, who
are, after all, depending on him to communicate information as clearly as possible.

As yo니 can see, they are punctuated just like clauses with the other s니bordinating conjunctions.
When the dependent clauses begin the sentence, they are set off from the independent clause

time clause as if it were a complete independent clause. Remember, it cannot work alone as it
does not express a complete tho니ght by itself.

One is that you will usually use the same tense in both closes:

However, if you are writing about the future, yo니 will only use the future tense in the

H 그러나 미래를 나타낼 때에는 주절에만 미래 시제를 사용한다.

Example D:
When I get home, I always take off my shoes.
My roommate usually calls his parents when he returns to the dormitory after a
weekend at home.
When we finish studying, we often go online to chat with o니r friends from home.

3-2 Adjective Clauses
As their name suggests, adjective cla니ses are dependent clauses that act as adjectives. In other
words, they are used to describe nouns or prono니ns. Adjective clauses begin with relative

pronouns or relative adverbs. For this reason, adjective clauses are also known as relative
clauses. Adjective clauses normally immediat이y follow the no니ns or pronouns they modify,
which are also known as their antecedents.

H 이 종속절은 명사나 대명사를 수식할 때 사용된다. 형용사절은 관계대명사나 관계부사로 시작한다. 이 때문에
형용사절을 관계산절이라고 하기도 한다. 형용사절은 일반적으로 명사나 대명사, 선행사 바로 뒤에 위치한다.

헤） Possessive Relative Pronoun

Whose is used to indicate possession. It replaces other possessive words. While it most often is
used in reference to people, it can also be used for things. IVhose cannot work alone and must
always work with a noun. As with the other relative pronouns, it needs to follow the antecedent.
In most relative clauses, the verb m니st agree with the relative pronoun's antecedent, b니t when
whae is the relative pronoun, the verb will agree with the noun that follows whose.

H 소유격 관계대명사 Whose는 소유 관계를 나타내기 위해 사용한다. 이것은 다른 소유격 단어를 대치한다. 주로
사람을 가리킬 때 사용되지만, 사물을 가리킬 수도 있다. whose는 단독으로는 사용할 수 없고 반드시 명사와 같이
사용해야 한다. 다른 관계대명사와 마찬가지로 선행사 뒤에 위치해야 한다. 대부분의 관계사절에서 동사는 관계대명
사의 선행사와 일치해야 하지만, whose가 관계대명사일 경우에는 whose뒤에 따르는 명사의 수에 따라 동사가 일
치하게 된다.

The noun that follows i사lose will serve as the subject of the adjective clause:

H 형용사절에서는 명사/대명사를 반복하지 않는다. 관계대명사가 원래 단어 대신 사용된다.
Incorrect: The man who he gave us a ride home lives near my grandmother.
Incorrect: The CD that it is playing right now is by my favorite artist
Incorrect: I have two classmates whose their father is a famous doctor.

2. Adjective clauses are dependent clauses and cannot work alone. Do not punctuate
them as if they were complete sentences.

Q 형용사절은 종속절이며, 혼자서는 문장을 구성하지 못한다. 완전한 문장처럼 구두점을 사용하면 안 된다.
Incorrect: The man who gave us a ride.
Incorrect: I have two classmates. Whose father is a famous doctor.

3. Keep the adjective clause as close to its antecedent as possible. This is especially
important if there is more than one no니n that you could be modifying in the sentence.

H 형용사절은 가능한 한 선행사와 가까운 곳에 위치해야 한다. 특히 문장 내에 수식할 수 있는 명사가 여러 개

@ Restrictive Versus Non-Restrictive Adjective Clauses
Now we are ready to talk about punctuating adjective

이auses.

So far we have only seen

examples of what are known as restrictive or essential clauses. The information given in them is
essential because it restricts the nouns or pronouns being modified. We need the information to
understand the sentence. If we omit the cla니se, we lose some of th은 meaning.

Q 물론 문장의 의미를 이해하기 위해서는 이러한 제한적 형용사절이 필요하다. 이 때문에 제한절의 경우에는

Non-restrictive adjective cla니ses use commas, and rather than identifying their antecedents,
they provide additional information about them. We use non-restrictive clauses when we are
writing about named people, places, and things, or when we are referring to something or
someone we've already mentioned.

H 비제한적 형용사절에서는 콤마를 사용하며, 선행사를 수식하는 것이 아니라 추가적인 정보를 제공할 뿐이다.

= Sun Valley, where I spent my vacation, is famous for its great ski resorts.

5. Bill Clinton was a popular president. + I voted for him twice.
= Bill Clinton, for whom I voted twice, was a popular president.

Common Errors of Korean Students

There are several points that Korean writers need to be aware of in order to avoid mistakes.
These errors are caused by interference of the Korean language, something that occurs nat니rally
when we learn a new language. Interference happens when we use a pattern from o니r own
lang니age in hopes that it will work in the other language, but it doesn't. The following essays
were written by Korean students, and they provide some good examples of specific areas that
you will want to be aware of and can work on to improve yo니r writing.

H 이제 한국인들이 흔히 하는 실수를 줄이기 위해 알아 두어야 할 몇 가지에 대해 알아 보자. 이러한 실수들은 한
국어와의 혼동 때문에 발생되는데, 다른 언어를 배울 때 자연스럽게 발생하는 현상들이다. 자신의 모국어에서 사용하
던 패턴이 다른 언어에서도 가능하다고 생각하기 때문에 혼동이 발생한다. 그러나 그렇지는 않다 다음 에세이는 한
국 학생이 작성한 것으로, 작문 실력을 향상시키기 위해 알아야 할 여러 가지 사항들이 들어 있는 좋은 예이다.

이ass

in about 45 minutes. She is making great progress in her 니se of English, b니t can yo니 find

some errors?

of articles (definite "the"

instead of indefinite "a/anz" articles used where none is needed, for example, "a lots"), missing

B. Word Form and Choice: In Korean, adverbs aren't derived from adjectives like in English
("finally"); not all Korean suffixes have English equivalents, and the different parts of speech in
English can be unclear for Korean speakers ("breath" vs. "br은athe"). Note the examples
numbered 3Z 4,9,12,18Z and 19, in which there are mistakes of these typ은s.

H 단어의 형태와 선택: 한국어에서는 영어처럼 부사("finally")가 형용사로부터 파생되지 않았다. 한국어 접미사

수 있다("breath" vs. "breathe"). 3, 4, 9,12,18,19번 문장에 이러한 에러들이 있다.

C. relative Cla니ses: In Korean, the modifiers come before the noun they modify, and this
difference in word order makes it difficult for Korean writers to get used to illative dauses and
the word order in English. Notice example #2 and the missing relative pronoun. However, also
notice the correct use of a illative clause in "when I meet at friend who is older..."

H 관계절: 한국어의 경우 수식어구가 수식 받는 명사 앞에 온다. 이러한 어순의 차이 때문에 영어의 어순이나
관계절에 익숙해지기 힘들다. 2번 문장을 보면 관계대명사가 생략되어 있다. 그러나 when I meet at friend
who is older..." 부분에서는 관계대명사를 올바르게 사용하고 있다.

D. Modals: Korean has three modal forms, can, must, and should, so the use of other modals and
their tenses in English can be difficult to learn. Notice ex리nple #14 and the incorrect modal
constnjction of the past tense ("couldn't found"). You need to remember that the helping verb
does the work and indicates past tense. The main verb does not change: "could find" is the
correct form.

찬가지다. 9번 문장의 경우가 이런 종류의 실수인 것에 유의하라. 9번 문장을 아마 이 학생은 "Through movies
and the imagination, we can do whatever we want and be whatever we want to be." 로 말한 듯하다.

올바른 전치사와 접속사를 골라 제대로 사용하는 것은 쉬운 일이 아니다. 아마도 이런 상세한 부분은 영어를 배우
는 단계에서 최후까지 남아 있을 문제다. 따라서 고급 단계에 올라가서도 계속해서 주의를 필요로 하는 일이다.

C. Sentence Structure: Notice example #1, a participial phrase that is missing a subject pronoun
and helping verb, something possible in Korean

이nce

the meaning would be clear from the

context. The passive ("They are stressed by...") is expressed by a suffix in Korean, so this mistake
is an obvious result of interference. This example follows the sophisticated first sentence with
two s니bordinate clauses, more evidence of the writer's increasing competence.

Q 문장 구조: 1 번은 주격 관계대명사와 조동사가 없는 불완전한 구이다. 한국어에서는 문맥에서 주어가 무엇임
을 알 수 있으므로 이런 문장이 가능하다. 수동태("They are stressed by...’)의 경우 한국어에서는 접미사를 통해
처리한다. 따라서 이런 실수는 명백히 두 언어 간의 차이에 따른 혼동의 결과이다. 이 문장 바로 앞에 있는 문장에
서는 2개의 종속절을 사용하였다. 이는 작성자의 언어 능력이 향상되었음을 나타내는 훌륭한 증거이다.

D. No Auxiliary Verbs: Unlike English, Korean does not use helping verbs, so their use can be
difficult to learn, especially when, in Korean, the subject is understood through the context and
the BE verb is not necessary. Notice examples #1 and 10, in which the verb BE is mis이ng.

H 조동사가 없다: 영어와 달리 한국어에는 조동사가 없다. 따라서 조동사의 용법을 배우기가 힘든데, 특히 문맥
을 통해서 주어를 찾거나 be 동사가 필요하지 않는 경우에는 더욱 그러하다. 1번과 10번을 보면 be를 빠트렸다.

이다. 콤마는 접속사 뒤가 아니라 앞에 있어야 한다. 이를 통해 작성자가 콤마의 필요성을 알고는 있지만 제대로
사용하고 있지는 않다는 것을 알 수 있다.

In brief, studies have shown that the most successful second language learners have a good
understanding of their own language. As you look at the sample essays above, ask yourself if
you understand how the structures work in Korean. Use what you already know abo니t your own
language to help you improve your English skills. Think abo니t the structures and work on
improving your understanding of how the languages differ. Be aware of the differences as well
as the similarities and use this knowledge to your advantage.

사람들이다. 앞에 나온 샘플 에세이를 살펴보면서 한국어의 구조가 어떠한지 이해했는가를 스스로에게 물어보자.
영어 실력 향상에 도움이 되도록 모국어에 대한 지식을 활용하자. 문장 구조에 대해 생각하고, 언어 간의 차이점에
대한 이해를 심화시키는데 활용하자. 유사성뿐만 아니라 차이점에 대해서도 잘 알고 활용할 수 있어야 한다.

다. 따라서 당신을 매니저와 같은 회사의 고위 간부가 될 만한 사람으로 인식하게 된다.

Writing persuasive essays is practice for this kind of situation. You consider a question a topic - you give your opinion, and you give logical reasons to support your opinion. This
is not an arbitrary form made up for school. It is a model for a way of thinking that is used
in business, and in all areas of life. So when you are learning to write essays, you are
learning to THINK in a certain way. Don't just focus on the words and grammar; focus on
the THINKING.

배우는 한 가지 방법이다.
• 단어나 문법에 얽매이지 말고 먼저 논리적으로 생각하는 것에 집중하자.

주제선정
Choosing a Topic

Often the topic will be provided by your teacher (or boss, s니pervisor, or co-workers).
Sometimes you will be asked to choose your own topic. How do you decide what to write
about? And what to say abo니t the topic?

Whenever possible, write about something that is important to you, something abo니t
which yo니 feel strongly. In Korea, sometimes students are not encouraged to have strong
opinions. They are expected to echo the opinions of their teachers and elders. This is not
true in Western cultures, particularly in the USA. There, students are expected to develop
their own opinions based on reason and insight. It does not matter whether your opinion is
"for" or "against"; it j니st matters that you have logical reasons to support it. So before you
write about a subject, think about it and develop a strong opinion.

Prewriting/Brainstorming

Once you have your topic, yen」need to decide what you want to say about it. Typically, this
is a process of "narrowing down." In other words, yo니 start with a broad topic and then
narrow it down to a more specific thesis. Ideally, you want to come up with fresh ideas that
interest you. There are several methods to help yen』do this.

• 주제가 결정되면 그 주제에 대해 무엇을 쓸 것인가를 결정하자.
• 주제를 넓은 의미에서부터 점점 좁혀 나가면서 창의적이고 흥미진진한 내용으로 구체적인 논제를 제시해 보자.

（丄）

Freewriting

Note the time and begin writing on your topic. For ten minutes, write anything that comes to
your mind about this topic. Do not stop to think. Do not censor anything. Do not worry about
spelling or grammar. Just keep writing. Let yo 니『thoughts flow freely. Many of yo 니 r tho 니 ghts will
not be relevant. But, by thinking and writing freely, some new ideas may come to you. You may
find a new approach to your topic.

In this example, the writer remembered something unexpected. He remembered that his 니rude

special insight, but if you give freewriting a chance, it may open your mind to new, fresh ideas.1’

가

Journal Writing

Keep a journal. Write in it every day. Write a few lines about your c니rrent essay topic. You may
have new thoughts today that you did not have yesterday. Since the journal is just to help you,
you can write anything you want. It will not be judged or graded, so yen」can write your true
thoughts and feelings.

• 최근의 에세이 주제에 관해 몇 줄이라도 쓰다 보면 어제 떠오르지 않은 생각을 오늘 가질 수도 있을 것이다. 따
라서 계속 매일 써 보자.
• 계속 기록하는 것 자체가 도움이 되기 때문에 무엇이든 원하는 것을 써 보자 이렇게 쓰는 것은 점수가 매겨지
거나 평가되는 것이 아니어서 진심에서 우러나오는 생각을 적을 수 있을 것이다.

Musicians practice mu이c every day. Football players practice football every day. If you are
serious about writing, write every day. The journal is the place to do this. When you get
comfortable with your jo니rnal, new ideas will come to you naturally while you are writing.

그

Listing and Clustering

Make a list of everything that comes to mind about your topic. Here is a sample list for an essay
about cameras:

Keeps memories
Digital vs. Film - which is better?
Capturing light
Composition
Give photos to your friends.
Give photos to strangers — good way to make new friends.
I hate to pose for photos — I always think I look bad.
Flash hurts my eyes.
Small cameras — big cameras - which is better?
Document my life with photos.

After you write the list, yo니 will want to think about it and decide which things you want to
discard and which ones you want to use in your essay.

Clustering is like listing, but it's not so neat. If you are like most people, you do not think in
straight lines. Your mind bounces in one direction, then another. Clustering reflects this kind of
thinking. To use ckistering, write down random elements and ideas about your topic and then
conn은ct them with lines and arrows. See how they go together. Notice how one idea leads to
the next.

Here are some other questions yo니 can ask about your subject:

What causes it? What does it cause to happen?
What is the purpose of it? What should be done about it?
What things are similar to it? What things are different?

For example, you might use these questions to help with an essay about the beach as follows:

Who went to the beach? Who was at the beach?
What was at the beach? What was the beach like?
When did you arrive at the beach? When did you leave?
Where did you go at the beach? Where was the beach located?

Why did you go to the beach? Why is the beach special?

ncountered at the beach.

은

Perhaps the most important question is:

What do I want to say about this subject?
The more detailed your answers, the more ideas they will generate.

:: Chart 1-Model for the Persuasive Essay

Introduction

Introduction Subject

This is not the only way to write an essay. It is a very simple, straightforward approach. If you can
master this form, it will provide you with a basis - a starting point - for writing essays that are
more advanced and more personal.

In yo니r persuasive essay, do not use one example and one fact for each reason. This
will make yo니r essay boring and predictable. Each time you present a reason, ask
yourself, "Would it be helpful to support this with an example or a fact?" In some cases
yo니 will 니드은 examples. In some cases you will use facts. In some cases you will use
both. And in some cases, a simple explanation will be enough. It depends on your
thesis and the point you are making. Don't do it the same way every time. Remember,
the Five Paragraph Model is just a guideline to help you think through the essay. It's
not a set of rules.

examples:

way the stock market works.

opportunity to be creative. Your introduction sets the tone of your essay. A good introd니ction
makes the reader want to read more.

To develop your the이s, start with your subject: love, money, health, football, new computer

Then ask yourself, now that I have considered this subject deeply, what is the main thing I want

■ Subject: Raising children
■ Narrower: Freedom and love vs. discipline and limits
■ Thesis Statement: To grow up properly, children need both love and discipline.

Once you have written a clear thesis statement, it is advisable to underline it. Though this is not
typical of published essays, it is valuable for school essays and in business writing. By
underlining your the외s, you are making sure that your reader (teacher, boss, co-worker) can
clearly identify the main point you are trying to make.

A

Bad There are lots of new diets on the market.

Here is a thesis statement that is not controversial: "In winter it is cold." This statement is true,

The following guidelines will help you to avoid weak thesis statements and to develop strong
thesis statements:

명확한 논제 진술을 위해 피해야 할 사항들

A thesis should not be obvious.
A thesis should not be a truism.
A thesis should not be empirically provable.
A thesis should be debatable.
A thesis should be clearly defined.
A thesis should not be too broad or vague.
A thesis should not be a question.
A thesis statement should not be a fragment.

4 f Explanations, Examples and Facts

A clear thesis resting on logical reasons is the basis for a good essay. But it is not enough. You
must explain your reasons so the reader can f니lly understand them, and you must s니pport them
with examples and facts. This support is the substance of your essay. It is extremely important,
and it is where most st니dents fail. If yo니 can learn to explain thoroughly and support your
explanations with examples and facts, you will be able to write excellent essays.

［ 血

3

• 지금까지 학습한 것처럼 좋은 에세이의 바탕에는 논리적인 이유들을 가진 명확한 논제가 있어야 한다. 그러나
그 이유들을 읽는 이에게 완전히 이해시킬 수 있는 예제와 사실에 대한 설명이 없으면 좋은 에세이라고 할 수
없다.
• 이 부분에서 많은 학생들이 제대로 전개를 하지 못해 에세이 쓰기에 실패한다. 예제와 사실을 바탕으로 설명해
나가는 방법을 배우게 된다면 좋은 에세이를 쓸 수 있을 것이다.

Suppose yo니 are writing a pers니asive essay with the following components:

■ Thesis:
All children should be taught to swim.

■ R 은 asons:
Children are safer if they know how to swim.

Your reader will naturally ask, "Safer in what way? How are children safer?" Do not assume the
reader knows the answer and do not make him guess. Spell it out. That means, explain it clearly,
step by step, so the reader is sure to understand. For example:

Bodies of water such as lakes, rivers, oceans, and swimming pools can be dangerous for
children. A child may fall into the water accidentally, or he may jump in, not realizing the
danger. When this happens, if the child does not know how to swim, he may die from
drowning. If he knows how to swim, he can survive by swimming to safety.

other words, you must tell where you got the facts so the reader can verify them.

The Writing Process - Fundamentals

In this case, the author is not presenting a fact; no one knows for sure whether there is life on
other planets, so no fact can prove this point. The statement presented is merely an opinion.
However beca니se it is the opinion of an authority with experience and credibility in this field, the
quote strengthens the author's thesis, thus making his essay more persua이ve.

Of course, not every point in every essay will be supported by examples and facts. A personal
essay abo니t pets or recreation will probably rely more on examples and less on statistics. An
essay about p니blic policy such as one about crime or the national budget will rely more on facts
and less on personal examples. Each time you make a major point in your essay, consider
supporting it with examples, facts, and a니thoritative guotes. Use your judgment to decide
whether to use facts, examples, both, or to reply solely on yo니r explanation.

Also, the way you arrange your explanations, examples and facts will change with each
paragraph and each point you are making. In some cases, the explanation will come first, and
examples and facts will follow. In other cases, yo니 may start with an example, and then explain
the example, thus illustrating your point. In other cases, the facts may come first. Whatever order
yo니 place them inz u이ng clear explanations, examples, and facts will make your essays very
strong and effective.

• 많은 사실들만 적어 놓고 구체적인 예가 없는 에세이는 썰렁하고 지겹게 느껴진다. 반대로 구체적인 예만 가지

Vocabulary Abusive: rough, vi이ent, cruel, harmf니

Transition in the Persuasive Essay
You have now gained knowledge of the main components of an essay, and yo니 are learning
how to work with them. But how do you move from one component to the next? How do you
go from the introduction to the first reason? From one reason to the next? From the final reason
to the conclusion?

To do this effectively you need transitions.

Transitions help your reader stay oriented and keep him from getting lost or confused as you
move from idea to idea. Clear transitions are essential to clear writing.

• 읽는 이에게 두 가지 생각에 대해 혼란을 주지 않으면서, 제대로 의미를 이어 주는 연결어는 에세이를 명확하게
만드는 데 중요한 역할을 한다.

Here is a sample excerpt from a pers니asive essay abo니t California:

It is easy to see that warm, sunny weather is one of the things that attract people to Southern
California. The snow piles up high on the nearby mountains, and everyone loves the great
skiing.

This seems strange and confusing. How can there be snow when it is sunny? This writer has
written two separate thoughts with no transition to show their relationship.

Here is the same excerpt with a transition added to connect the two ideas:

It is easy to see that warm, sunny weather is one of the things that attract people to So니 them
California.

Q 따스하고 쾌적한 날씨가 사람들이 남부 캘리포니아에 반하는 이유 중 하나이다

들에는 눈이 많이 쌓여 있고, 많은 사람들이 멋진 스키를 즐긴다.

The transition

이arifies

the relationship between the two statements. One tells about California

when the weather is warm, while the other tells about California in the winter. The passage is no
longer confusing; it is easy for the reader to follow.

In any persuasive essay, whenever you are going from one id은a to another idea, write a
transition that will help your reader to follow.

In most essays, the transitions come naturally. However, if you are stuck for a transition, you may
find it helpful to use simple standard phrases such as:

Another reason is ...
Another benefit of...
Another advantage of...
It is also true that...
Additionally ...
Moreover...
On the other hand ...
There are many reasons that can contribute to__ . One such reason is ...
There are other possible causes to___. One possible cause is ...
Another problem critics point out is ,.,
Another type of____ is ...
Since this is true, it follows that...
It is easy to see that...
Writing transitions is like taking the reader's hand and leading him through your experiences
and thoughts. It allows the reader to relax and feel confident that he will be able to fully
understand you. Witho니t transitions, the ideas and events in an essay seem disconnected, and
the reader has to struggle to figure out how they go together. With clear transitions, the reader
has no trouble following the ideas and events, so he can easily understand and appreciate yo니r
writing.

• 연결어는 읽는 이의 손을 잡고 그를 글쓴이의 생각과 경험 속으로 이끄는 역할을 한다. 따라서 명확한 연결어는
읽는 이가 에세이의 내용과 사건을 끊어지지 않게 해서 읽는 이로 하여금 쉽게 글을 이해하고 바르게 판단할 수
있게 만들어 준다.

（호 Conclusions
Finish yo니r essay with a concluding paragraph. Typically, you will restate your the이s in a
different way. You may mention some of your major points again to "wrap them up." Usually it is
not a good idea to introduce an entirely new idea or new train of thought in your conclusion.
However, yo니 may add a simple statement that relates to the future of your subject such as a
prediction

about

the

f니Hire,

a

provocative

question,

or

a

statement

that

reinforces

the

importance of the subject. The purpose of your concluding paragraph is to remind the reader of
your thesis, reinforce the idea that the thesis is correct, and let the reader know that the essay
has come to an end. A good concluding paragraph has a scene of finality, and leaves the reader
with a positive feeling about the essay.

Editing
k-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- df

You have written your essay from beginning to end. B니t yo니 are not finished. Now it is time
to stop thinking as a writer and start thinking as an editor.
While yo니 are writing the essay, yen』should not worry abo니t spelling or grammar. You
should be foe니sing on your ideas and how to comm니nicate them. But when you go into the
editing phase, yo니 m니st look at your essay objectively. Search for errors, and carefully
correct the spelling and grammar.
First, check the words for spelling mistakes. If yen』wrote yo니r essay on a computer, you can
run a spell check, b니t remember, spell checks miss many errors. For example, if yo니 confuse
words such as "their" and "there," "its" and "it's," or "bear" and "bare," the spell check will not
catch yo니r errors. Therefore, even after you run the spell check, yo니 need to check your
essay yourself, searching for any words that may be confused or misspelled.
Next check for grammatical errors. Again, you may use a grammar check program on your
comp니ter, tan remember these programs are flawed. Do not rely on them too much. Learn
to check yo니r own writing for errors. To do this, it is very h이pful if you know what kinds of
errors you have made in the past. For example, if yo니 have a problem with run-on
sentences, check your essay for long sentences. When you find sentences that seem too
long, check to make sure that yo니 haven't joined two complete sentences without a joining
word or appropriate punct니ation.

If you have had problems with sentence fragments, then check the sentences in your essay to
make sure each one has a subject and predicate (verb). Also make sure that subordinate clauses
are always attached to independent clauses.

If yo니 have had problems with tenses, check your essay to see if you are using tenses
consistently. If you are in the past tense, stay in the past tense. If you are in the present tense,
stay in the present. Do not mix them up inappropriately.

Article errors are a very common problem. Check your essay for articles (a, an, the) and mark any
spots you think may need an article or may have an unnecessary article. If possible, get help
from a teacher, tutor, or friend who is a native English speaker. Show him the spots you have
marked and ask if they need artkies. Use this same method for checking prepo이tions. Look
through your essay for words like "at," "to," "of," "from," and "in." If you are not sure that you have
used these words correctly, mark them and check with your teacher, tutor, or friend. Do not feel
badly if you have trouble with articles and prepositions. Almost everyone who learns English has
difficulty with these kinds of words. Mastering them is mostly a process of trial and error.

• 문법 교정을 할 때에는 주어와 동사의 연결이 제대로 되었는지, 동사의 시제는 일치하는지, 관사를 제대로 표시
했는지, 전치사는 제대로 사용했는지를 확인해야 한다. 이런 문법적인 문제는 모든 사람들에게 쉽지 않은 것이기
때문에 잘못 사용하는 것에 부끄러워하지 말고 답이 확실하지 않다고 느낄 경우에는 가능한 한 주위의 모든 아
는 사람들에게 확인해 보자. 이런 시행착오 속에서 영어 문법을 정복해 가는 것이다.

Check your transitions. Have you moved from one idea to the next in a clear way that is easy for
the reader to follow?

The Writing Process - Fundamentals

Finally, look for awkward sentences. Read yo니r essay aloud, slowly. The sentences should flow
naturally, almost musically. If the sentences seem choppy, overly complicated, twisted, or hard
to follow, rework them. Look for simpler, more direct ways to make your points. Editing is usually
a process of paring down, getting rid of extra words and phrases, simplifying expressions, and
finding the natural flow of the lang니age.

■ 마지막으로 의미가 통하지 않는 문장을 찾아 보자. 만약 문장의 흐름에서 자연스럽지 못하거나 복잡하고 이해하
기 어려운 문장이라고 생각되면 다시 써 보자.
■ 간단하면서도 직설적으로 중요 의미를 전달할 수 있게 하자.
쓸데없는 말이나 문구들을 잘 다듬어서 자연스러운 문장이 될 수 있게 하는 것이 교정의 참 의미이다.

For more abo니t revising and editing, see Chapter 8.

Vocabulary Awkward: clumsy; lacking grace
Choppy: rough and lacking smoothness in flow; irreg니lar, shifting and changing

NOTE This essay is presented in five paragraphs to demonstrate the five paragraph form. However,

when you write an essay that is this long, you will probably want to divide it into more than five
paragraphs. When a paragraph gets too long, look for natural places to break it up. For instance, a

facts or examples would still be to support one of your reasons or main points.

There is nothing special about five paragraphs, nor about three reasons; this is just a
convenient way to think about a simpl은 essay. The important thing is to include the
main components:

Q 다섯 문단과 세가지 이유에 관해서는 특별한 규칙 같은 것은 없다. 이것은 단지 간단한 에세이를 떠
올릴 때 사용하는 편리한 방법일 뿐이다. 더 중요 한 것은 다음의 주요한 요소가 있어야 한다는 것이다

• Introduction with thesis
논제를 제시한다.

■ Reasons or main points to support the thesis
이유나 주요 포인트로 논제를 뒷받침한다.

* Conclusion.
결론을 맺는다.

If the essay has these components, you will have the basis for an eff은ctiv은 essay. It
doesn't matter how many paragraphs or how many reasons it has.

Q 만약 에세이가 이런 요소를 가지고 있다면, 효과적인 에세이의 기본을 갖추는 것이다. 그럴 경우에
는 몇 문단으로 쓰든지 몇 개의 이유를 가지고 있는지는 중요하지 않다.

This kind of account is called a narrative. A narrative is "a continuo니s acco니nt of the particulars
of an event or transaction; a story."1 A narrative typically answers the question, "What
happened?" The ability to tell what happened in a clear, effective way is valuable in business,
family, and every aspect of life. This is why we learn to write narratives when we study writing.
We practice narrative writing by creating narrative essays.

When you are assigned to write a narrative essay, typically you will be asked to

1. Tell what happened in logical order, step-by-step. Include and highlight the most
important things that happened so the reader can follow the train of events. Don't let
irrelevant information get in the way.

B

2. Set each scene in your narrative by describing the people, places, and objects as they
come into the story

Thesis

Persuasive essays are based on a thesis. A narrative essay may also have a the외s, depending
upon the assignment and what you want to achieve with the essay. Using a thesis can be very
helpful in unifying yo니r narrative and helping you to decide what to include and what to leave
out. In most cases, it is advisable to unify your narrative essay with a clear the외s.

• 나레이티브 에세이에서는 명확한 하나의 논제에 대해 필요 없는 내용은 빼고 필요한 것은 더 첨가하는 것과 같
이 논제에 대해 내용을 일체화시켜 나가는 과정이 필요하다.

In the previous example of the essay title "New Year in Cambodia/' the thesis of the essay might
be:

Q Thesis A:

Holiday celebrations are a valuable tool for helping people to come together
and bond into a cohesive community.

Or it may be:

O Thesis B:

Often we think distant peoples are very different from ourselves, sometimes
frighteningly so. However, traveling to foreign lands teaches 니s that, people are
essentially the same everywhere.

H 우리는 지방에 사는 사람들과 우리가 아주 다르다고 종종 생각한다. 물론 그런 면이 있기도 하지만, 그런 지
방을 여행하고 교류를 하게 되면 '인간의 깊은 내면은 만인이 공통적이다,는 것을 똑바로 알게 된다.

These are both good thesis statements. Each would be developed in a different way. If you used

Transitions in the Narrative Essay

Like persuasive essays, narrative essays use transitions. The transitional phrases in narrative
essays help your reader follow the train of events; they cue the reader so he knows when yo니r
story is moving from one place to another, from one time to another, or from one occurrence to
another. Most often the transition lets the reader know when the next event occurred; it places
the event in time. Here are some simple typical transitional phrases that you might use in a
narrative essay:

표

■

연결어는 나레이티브 에세이를 읽는 동안 이야기의 흐름이 한 장소에서 다른 장소로 옮겨 가거나, 이 시간에서

다른 시간으로 바뀌거나, 이 사건에서 다른 사건으로 옮겨가는 것과 같이 사건의 흐름을 연결시켜 줘 읽는 이로
하여금 자연스럽게 이해할 수 있게 도와 준다.

When someone shows strong emotions, they will be thought of as a strong and
brave person. For example, last Saturday I went with my friend Theary to the
Parkway Shopping Mall. We were on the third floor, which is kind of dingy and
quiet; there were not many people aro니nd. While we were looking at jeans in a
boutique window, we saw another girl named Seang Len who owed Theary five
dollars. Seang Len is a big girl with a hot temper, while Theary is small and meek.
When Theary politely asked Seang Len to pay back the money she owed, Seang
Len got angry and wanted to fight. Theary was afraid and asked me to help her. I
talked to Seang Len and asked for the money back. I told her that threatening
Theary was not okay. At first, Seang Len didn't want to pay the money back; she
claimed the amount was too small. I replied that I would tell the teacher if she
didn't pay the money back. I talked to her in a very strong voice with strong
emotions. When she saw this, she was afraid and she decided to pay the money
back to Theary. This taught me that showing strong emotions could lead to
successful results. Most importantly, it kept my friend from being hurt.
I had a similar experience last year when I was riding my motorbike along the
street with my friend Dalin. It was on a Sunday afternoon at a busy traffic circle in
the middle of the city. There were so many cars, trucks, and motorbikes, engines
growling, emitting smoke and kicking up dust. Suddenly another motorbike rode
straight toward me and crashed into me. Fortunately, my friend and I were not
h니rt and my motorbike was okay. However, the man who crashed into us was
angry beca니se his motorbike was damaged. He was a tall man with a mean face
and a tattoo on his arm. He demanded money from my friend to fix his motorbike.
I said, "It wasn't o니r fault, so why should we pay you?" He became angrier and
more demanding. I stood my ground and talked to him in a very strong voice with
strong emotions. I added that if he still needed money, we could ask the police to
solve the problem. When I said that, he looked scared. Suddenly he said it was
okay, and he allowed my friend and me to go away safely. My strong emotions had
protected us from the man.
In condition, when a person is in trouble, showing strong emotions can be a
good way to solve the probl이n and stay safe. My advice to anyone is, don't be too
meek. Stand 니p for yourself and let people know how you fe은I. That is the path to
confidence and success.

This essay was successful because it told about a series of events clearly, step by step. It is easy
to follow the story and to understand what happened. There is plenty of detail, so we can
picture the events, and we can easily see why these events were meaningful.

Learning to write narratives is very important. It is also very rewarding. Communicating the
experiences and events of your life makes you feel more connected to other people. When you
meet people in daily life, they have only a superficial knowledge of you, but when they know
about your past and the things that yo니 have lived thro니gh, then they gain a deeper
understanding of you and what yo니r life is all abo니t.

Remember, narratives are stories, and people all over the world have always loved to tell and
hear stories. This chapter has given you a way to begin your work on narrative essays by
arranging the events in chronological order, then setting the scene and describing the action as
you move from one event to the next. Chapter 7 will take a more advanced approach to
narratives, helping yo니 to create detailed descriptions and lively metaphors that will enrich your
narrative writing.
