Sched니le specific times to write. For example, you might designate Monday,
Wednesday, and Friday mornings, 9 a.m. to 10:30 a.m., as your writing times. Or
esday and Thursday evenings. Or every night for an hour before yen』go to 이eep.

'「니

Whatever time fits your schedule. The important thing is to set definite times and
stick to them. Many st니dents intend to write, but they just don't get aro니nd to it. They
wait until an assignment is almost due, and then th은y try to write it very quic시y.
We've all known st니dents who brag about ’'cramming" for a test or writing an
assignment at the last minute, staying up all night to get a passing grade. But these
st니dents often fail in the long r니n. Th은 truth is, over time, students who do their
assignments at th은 last minute simply cannot compete with st니dents who know how
to schedule their time effectively. St니dents who set aside times to work on their
writing do more than simply complete their assignments: They have the time to think
deeply about their subjects, to experiment and explore the writing process, and
ultimately to learn as much as possible from 은ach assignment. Use your writing time
to work on writing assignments as well as journal writing, freewriting, and writing
abo니t s니bjects that interest you. If yo니 do this regularly, yo니 will start to look forward
to your writing times; yo니 will think of this time as a welcome period to explore and
develop ideas, remember and retell experiences, and express yourself in a variety of
satisfying ways.

H 작문할 시간을 구체적으로 정해 놓기 바란다. 예를 들어 월, 수, 금 오전 9시부터 10시 30분까지 1

시간 반 동안을 작문 시간으로 정할 수도 있고, 화요일이나 목요일 저녁 또는 매일 자기 전 1시간을 할
애할 수도 있다. 아무 시간이나 스케줄에 맞는 시간을 정해서 하는 것이다. 여기서 중요한 것은 일정한
시간을 정한 뒤 꼭 지키는 것이다. 많은 학생들이 작문을 하려고 생각을 하기는 하지만 막상 하지는 못
하고 있다가, 과제 제출 마감 시한이 거의 다 되어서야 허겁지겁 작문을 한다. 시험 공부나 작문 숙제를
벼락치기로 밤새도록 해서 간신히 낙제를 면할 점수를 얻었다고 떠벌리는 학생들을 많이 보았을 것이다.
그러나 이런 학생들은 결국은 실패하기 마련이다. 이런 학생들은 시간을 효율적으로 안배할 줄 아는 학
생들을 당해 낼 수 없다. 작문할 시간을 따로 챙겨 놓는 학생들은 단순히 주어진 과제만 하는 것이 아니
다. 주제에 대해 깊게 생각하고, 작문 과정에 대해서 연구하고 탐구하다 보면, 과제로부터 최대한 많은
것을 배우게 된다. 작문 과제뿐만 아니라 일기 작성이나 자유 각문 관심 분야에 대한 작문도 시간을 할
애해야 한다. 규칙적으로 하다 보면 작문 시간을 기다리게 될 것이다. 즉, 이 시간을 어떤 생각들을 탐구
하고 발전시키고, 경험에 대해 기억하고 재 서술하면서, 여러 가지 만족할 수 있는 방법으로 자신을 표
현하는 시간으로 여기게 될 것이다.

Other Types of Essays
北'

Persuasive and narrative essays are the most common types of essays, but there are many
other kinds. One of the most important types is the literary essay, which distills and
interprets literature. Literary essays will be discussed in more detail in Chapter 7.

Each type of essay requires a somewhat different approach, but most of the fundamentals
you learned for writing pers니asive essays will still apply. Introd니ce yo니r subject. Make clear
points about yo니r subject. Support your points with clear explanations, examples, and facts.
End with a concl니이on. Wherever it is appropriate, have a single main point and organize
yo니r essay to support that main point. If yen』are telling about events, use the techniq니es
yo니 learned for writing narrative essays.

에세이 작성에 공통적으로 적용된다.
• 주제를 소개하고 주제에 관한 분명한 포인트를 잡고, 명확한 설명, 예제, 그리고 사실들로 주제를 뒷받침하고
결론을 맺는다.

How to Essay

This type of essay tells how to do a particular task. For example, it might explain how to ride a
bicycle, how to build a house, how to cook bibinba, or how to climb a mountain. Typically, how­
to essays give step-by-step instr니ctions. They are meant to be practical, helpful documents.
Learning to write how-to essays has practical application in bu외ness, where it is often necessary
to teach new employees how to do specific tasks from using a copy machine to running
shipping and receiving department.

Personal Essay

In this type of essay, you tell about your personal life, sharing your thoughts and feelings.

Cause and Effect Essay
These essays demonstrate and illuminate cause and effect relationships. For example, the essay
might show how a cause:
Putting too much pressure on children to do well in school

Can lead to effects:
Children having personal and emotional problems

Or how a cause:
Over-valuation of the won

Can lead to effects:
Decreased exports, increased imports, loss of jobs, recession

Research Essay

This could be a persuasive essay, a comparison/contrast essay, or any other kind of essay that

facts and information to help the reader understand the subject and to support his thesis. The
sources of the information m니st be clearly accurate and identified.

Sample Essay
An essay can often fit into more than one category. This is a sample comparison/
contrast essay that whimsically contrasts and compares croissants with donuts. It is
also a five-paragraph persuasive essay that tries to persuade the reader that donuts
are better than croissants. See if you can identify the introduction, thesis, reasons,
examples, facts, and conclusion. Also note how the essay shows the similarities and
differences between donuts and croissants.

Pastries are bad for you. They make you fat and they give you bad skin.
But we love them anyway. The French croissant is probably the world's bestknown

pastry.

Because

it

is

light,

buttery,

and

subtle,

many

people

believe

the croissant is the best pastry of all. But these people are deluded. In fact,
the

American

donut

is

much

better.

Why?

Three

reasons.

Holes,

crumbs,

and color.
Croissants have points. They have curves. They have something like a dent
in the middle. But they don't have holes. What is a hole? It is nothing. It is the
absence

of

something.

When

you

eat

the

donut, the

hole

disappears.

Where

did it go? Was it ever really there? Or did you just imagine it? Holes are
philosophical conundrums. They are enigmas. You can puzzle them out as you
eat, feeling the tug of the universal mystery and the simultaneous satisfaction
of the sweet, unbelievably fattening donut. The mystery of the donut hole is so
grand, so epic, that many American donut stands have built one-hundred foot
plaster donuts that loom over the adjoining streets and sidewalks, teasing with
their mystery and calling local epicures to come and get another old fashioned.
Or a dozen. You can look through a donut hole.

Subtle: d 이 icate
Delude: deceive or mi이ead the mind
Crumbs: very small pieces broken off from baked foods such as a cookie, cake, or bread.

Absurd: silly, ridiculous, or unreasonable

In this chapter, you have learned a basic approach to persuasive and narrative essays, and

of

that

are

learning

in

chapters

are

important,

persuasive essay is especially vital. Both the TOEFL and SAT tests require students to
write persuasive essays, and most major American universities use persuasive essays to
evaluate new students. These institutions use persuasive essays because they are a standard
form of communication in education, business, and in civic life. Mastering the skills used
in persuasive essays is essential to getting good grades on the TOEFL Writing Test, the
SAT Writing Test, and to passing university placement examinations. But it is much more
than that. Learning to write persuasive essays will be of essential value to you as you go
forward in your education, your business career, and your life as a citizen and a member of
the international community.

Being Specific

Specificity.
Being specific is arguably the most important single skill you will learn as you build your writing
skills.
One of the most common comments that teachers make on essays is, "Be more specific." Many
st니dents do not 니nderstand what this comment means. Is it just some strange idea that teachers
have in school?

In fact, specificity is not an academic concept. It is something you deal with every day.

• 내용을 구체화 하는 방법을 배우는 것은 작문 공부에서 가장 중요한 기술을 배우는 것이다.
• 미국 학교에서 에세이를 제출한 후에 학생들이 가장 많이 받는 조언은 "더 구체적으로 쓰라,,는 것이다.
• ’구체적’이란 것은 교수만이 가자는 특이한 생각이라고 여길 만큼 학생 다수가 그 의미를 정확하게 이해하지 못한다

Suppose your friend says to you, "I went to a party this weekend. It was so great. I've never been
to such a wild party!"

Q 당신의 친구가 "지난 주에 간 파티는 정말 끝내주었어 그렇게 광란의 파티는 경험해 본 적이 없었어" 라는 말을
했다고 가정해 보자.

You naturally ask, "What happened at the party?"

H 당연히 당신은 "그 파티에서 무슨 일이 있었는데?" 라고 묻게 된다.
When you ask, "What happened?" you are asking your friend to be more specific. You want your
friend to provide specific details. Why? So that you can picture the party and understand what
took place.

H "무슨 일이 있었는데?" 라고 물었을 때, 당신은 친구에게 좀더 구체적인 것을 요청하고 있는 것이다. 즉, 좀더 구체
적인 세부 사항을 알기를 원하는 것이다. 그렇게 해야지만 그 파티에서 무슨 일이 있었는지 이해하고 상상할 수 있기
때문이다.

Being specific is helpful in your day-to-day life and is extremely important in business and at

• 일상의 대화 속에서 발생했던 일에 대해 얘기할 때 구체적으로 설명하기 전에는 듣는 이가 제대로 판단할 수 없
는 경우가 많다.

것이다. 즉, 이러한 구체적인 정보는 의사 소통을 위한 아주 중요한 핵심이다.

Vocabulary Concept: idea
Frustrated: irritated or annoyed
Percussion: instruments that are hit to produce a sound such as drums, cymbals, and tambourines

What does it mean to be specific? We know that being specific is the opposite of being general.
But if you write a particular statement, how do yen』know if it is specific or general?

O Exam 이 e A:

Korea has many beautiful temples.

Is this statement specific or general?

The answer is neither. By itself, the statement is neither general nor specific because specificity is
"illative." In most cases, we can only call a statement specific or general in illation to other
statements.

Consider this statement:

Q Example B:

Asia has many beautiful temples.

This statement is more general than Example A. Since Korea is part of A외a, Asia is a larger
category than Korea. Therefore, referring to Asia is more general than referring to Korea.

This statement "Asia has many beautiful temples" is true, but it leaves the reader wondering,
"Which parts of Asia? Which temples?"

The previous statement "Korea has many bea니tiful temples" is more specific, but it still leaves
the reader wondering, "Which parts of Korea?"

O Example C:

Kyongsang Province has many beautiful temples

Kyongsang Province is a smaller category than Asia or Korea, so this statement is more specific.
But it still leaves the reader wondering, "Which temples?"

O Example D:
The Pulguksa Temple in Kyongju is beautiful.

This is a very specific statement. It refers to one specific temple. The reader knows precis이y what
the writer is referring to.

More Specific

더 구체적인 진술

When you write, "Hamburgers are good with ketchup," you are referring to a category called
hamburgers. This category is very large, in이uding billions of hamburgers. The category of
sandwiches is even larger; it in이니des all hamburgers, plus fish sandwiches, cheese sandwiches,
and every other kind of sandwich. "Things you eat" is a vast category inch』ding every kind of
food.

"McDonald's hamburgers" is a smaller category than hamburgers, but it is still a huge category,
inci니ding billions of hamburgers. The hamburgers at one particular McDonald's is a much
smaller category; it in이udes thousands of hamburgers. When you write about one single
hamburger that yo니 ate at one particular time, then yo니 are being very specific. The smaller the
category you write about, the more specific is your writing.

Note the statement at the top of the list: "Things go good with other things." This kind of
statement is so general it conveys almost no information. Statements that are more specific
usually help your reader understand you more clearly.

The Writing Process - Beyond Fundamentals

Does this mean you should always make specific statements and never make general
statements?

Of course not. General statements are also essential to good writing. It is extremely important to
understand this:

Good communication typically goes back and forth between

O A] General Ideas （포괄적 의미의 일반 진술）

and

O B] Specific Details and Exam이es （구체적인 세부사항과 예제들）

The main points you are trying to convey in your essays are usually general ideas. To write
effectively, each time you present a general idea, you support it with more specific explanations
and details. If you balance the general with the specific, your essays will be clear and effective.

• 구체적인 진술이 중요하기는 하지만 그렇다고 항상 모든 문장이 구체적인 진술이어야 하는 것은 아니다.
• 포괄적 의미의 일반적인 진술도 좋은 글을 위해서는 필요하다.
■ 좋은 의사 소통 방식은 일반적인 견해는 물론 구체적인 사항과 예를 적절히 사용하는 것이다.
• 작문을 할 때에도 일반적인 견해를 펼치다가 구체적인 설명과 예를 적절히 덧붙인다면 글의 의미는 분명하고 효
과적으로 전달될 것이다.

To illustrate this, s니ppose you write the following statement:

O Exam이e A: 구체적인 진술
The football team from Kyunggi High School had two star players, but they lost the
championship game this year. Our school didn't have any star players, but we won the
championship by using a series of five player passes and intricate combination plays.

This is a fairly specific statement because it refers to specific teams playing specific games for a
specific championship.

The Advanced Persuasive Essay

What is the difference between an advanced essay and a basic essay?

Like a basic essay, an advanced persua이ve essay will have an introduction and a thesis; it will
support the thesis with reasons that are in turn supported by examples and facts, and it will end
with a conclusion. However, an advanced essay will go deeper into the subject. It will be richer.
The examples will be more detailed, the facts more extensive. If it is successful, an advanced
essay will b니ild a stronger, more convincing case to persuade the reader.

How do you structure an advanced essay?

Once again, there is no single correct way. Each writer will take his own individ니al approach.
However, the following twelve-step model may help you to plan an advanced essay. It is not
necessary for you to follow each one of these steps. For some subjects, step two may be very
important, but step nine will not be helpful. For other subjects, it will be the opposite. It depends
upon your subject and your thesis.

It is also not necessary that the steps be placed in this order; this is just one possible sequence.
In other words, this is not a form that you must copy and follow; rather, it is a jumping off point,
a way to think through your advanced essay.

■

고급 단계의 에세이가 일반적인 수준의 에세이와 다른 점은 주제에 대해 좀더깊이 접근 하면서 더 많은 구체적

인 예제를 제시한다는 것이다.
■

이런 고급 단계의 에세이를 쓰게 되면 당연히, 읽는 이에게 더 강한 신뢰와 설득력을 주게 된다.

• 아래 표3을 기준으로 고급 단계의 에세이 쓰는 방법에 대해 알아보자.

다. 따라서 꼭 이 순서대로 따라야 하는 것은 아니지만 고급 단계의 에세이를 깊고 충분하게 사고하게 하는 하나
의 출발점이자 방법이다.

tart 3 - Model for the Advanced Essay

1. Introduce S니bject / Establish Common Ground
(Concerns that you share with your reader)

2. Establish Compelling Need (or desirability)
(Why is it important to deal with this subject?)

3. Thesis
(Advanced methods for developing your thesis statement)

4. Key Premises
(Check for key premises that your thesis rests on. Give support where advisable.)

5. Context
(Historical, philosophical, cultural, schools of thought)

6. Reason #1
(Explanations. Examples. Support.)

7. Reason #2
(Explanations. Examples. Support.)

8. Reason #3
(Explanations. Examples. Support.)

Or suppose your topic is ed니cation and you make this claim:

O Thesis E:
We should raise taxes to pay for more and better education throughout Korea.
H 한국의 더 나은 교육 환경을 위해 세금을 인상해야 한다.

People who do not have children might object. They might think:

I don't have children, why should I pay more taxes to educate other peoples' children?
Let the mothers and fathers pay to educate their own children.

Rather than ignoring these childless people, you try to establish common ground with them. For
example, you might point out that having educated citizens benefits everyone. The student who
you support today, may one day develop a new medical technique that saves your life.

In other words, think about people who might not respond to your argument and see if you can
find a way to include them and connect with them. You may do this in the introduction to your
essay or at any point where it would be h은Ipful.

If you do not establish common ground, the reader may feel uncomfortable, as if you ar은 trying
to force your ideas 니pon him. Establishing common ground makes the reader feel that you are
on the same side, exploring common interests and solving shared pro비ems.

Essay writing is a type of p니blic discourse. We are exploring interests that are shared by many
other people. Establishing common gro니nd reminds the reader that we are engaged in this
process together.

Establish Compelling Need (or Desirability)

Suppose this is your thesis:

O Example A: 논제 제시

The population of Korea is declining. Therefore we must encourage Korean couples to
have more children.

Perhaps the reader agrees with you. He accepts that the population is declining and agrees that
it may be a good idea for couples to have more children. But he may think:

O Exam이e B: 반응 1

This statement is true, but it is not very important; it's not a big problem, so I don't
need to take time to think abo니t this.

In this case, the reader will not be interested in reading your essay; he will not care abo니t it very
much.

To solve this problem, try to convince your reader that what you are discussing is very important
and needs to be dealt with. This is called 'Establishing compelling need or desirability." In other
words, you answer the question: "Why is my topic important?" For example, you might offer the
following explanation:

O Example C: 논제에 대한 설명

If the population of Korea continues to decline, there will not be eno니gh people to
work at the important jobs. We will not have eno니gh scientists and engineers to
develop new products, so we will be 니nable to compete in the global marketplace.

Now I understand why this is an important issue. Let's see what the writer has to say
about it.

Once the reader sees the importance of the subject, he will be more interested in reading your

In the following essay about gun control, American Senator Edward Kennedy establishes
compelling need by using statistics:

O Example F: 통계를 사용해 필요성을 부각시킨 사례 2
Every 50 minutes an American is killed by a handg니n; 29 Americans who are alive
today will be shot dead tomorrow. Handguns will take 10,000 lives this year and will
threaten or wo니nd another 250,000 citizens. There are 55 million handguns in
circulation. The lethal number rises by two and a half million each year. By the year
2000, there will be 100 million handguns in America. ["The Need for Gun Control
Legislation," Los Angeles Times, April 15,1981]

These statistics help establish the compelling need for a solution to the handgun problem in
America.

There are some arguments in which it is particularly important to establish compelling need. For
example, if your essay calls for your co니ntry to give foreign aid to another nation, many readers
would be likely to respond:

There are many problems in the world. We cannot intervene to solve every problem in
every foreign co니ntry. Why is THIS partic니ar problem so important?

An effective essay wo니d anticipate this question and explain why this partic니ar country has a
need that is more extreme or in some other way especially compelling.

Not every persuasive essay is based on compelling need. For example, in an essay advocating
support for space exploration, you might frankly admit that exploring space is not necessary. In
this case, however, instead of showing compelling need, yo니 co니Id demonstrate compelling
desirability: we may not need to explore space, but it is extremely desirable because of all the
benefits we will gain if we do so.

Establishing compelling need is not a rule or req니irement, b니t it is a component of many
successful essays. As the writer you must exercise yo니r judgment in deciding when it is
appropriate to establish compelling need and how best to do it.

If you decide to establish compelling need, where should this be placed in yo니r 은ssay? Logical

3 Advanced Methods for Developing Your Thesis Statement

The thesis is the most important part of the essay. It is the core of your essay, yo니r main point.
Everything else is based on the thesis. In the last chapter, we discussed ba외c ways to develop a
thesis. Here we will look at the the이s in more detail, presenting two methods to develop a thesis
for an advanced pers니asive essay: the fo니r step method and the syllogism method.

3-1 The Four Step Method

Step f 핵심 주장
Ask, "What am I really trying to say?" or "What do I want the reader to agree with?" If the reader
says, "He's right, we should do X," what is X?

Write the answer as a statement. Make the statement as clear and direct as possible:

3-2 The Syllogism Method
This is a classical method based on formal logic. Formal logic is a system of thinking and
communicating that was developed in Greece over two thousand years ago. A central concept
of formal logic is the syllogism. The thesis for your essay can be based on a syllogism.

What is a syllogism?

A syllogism is a form of deductive reasoning, that is, reasoning which moves from the general to
the specific. Each syllogism consists of a major premise, a minor premise, and a concision. A
premise is a proposition upon which an argument is based. In any syllogism, if both premises are
correct, then it follows that the conclusion must be correct. Syllogisms have particular appeal to
writers with backgrounds in mathematics and other sciences. If you find syllogisms very difficult,
don't worry: You can always develop thesis statements by

니sing

the other methods you have

learned here. However, if you are familiar with classical logic and/or you like to think in a
mathematical way, you may find syllogisms helpful as you develop yo니r essays.

• 일반적인 내용에서 구체적인 내용을 제시하는 연역적인 형태가 'syllogism' （ 3단 논법）이다.
• 3단 논법은 대 전제와 소 전제 그리고 결론으로 구성되어 있다. 또한 이 전제들은 주장하고자 하는 논거를 바탕

으로 하는 표현된 생각이다.
• 대 전제와 소 전제가 맞는다면 따르는 결론은 반드시 맞아야 하는 3단 논법은 수학이나 과학을 배운 사람들에게
특히 좋은 방법이다.

QSample syllogisms: 3단 논법의 예제

이

:: Chart4-Sam

eSyllogisms

[Major Premise]

[Minor Premise]

[Conclusion]

대전제

소전제

결론

All guests are w이come here.

You are a guest.

Therefore, you are welcome here.

Plants need water

Ferns are plants

Therefore, ferns need water.

All humans are mortal

Mi-La is human.

Therefore, Mi-La is mortal.

The major premise is general, typically relating to a broad category. The minor premise is more
specific: It relates to an individual, or a smaller category. If you can prove the major and minor
premises, then the condusion necessarily follows.

• 대 전제는 넓은 부류의 일반적인 사항이고, 소 전제는 개인적이면서 좁은 부류이며 좀더 구체적이다.

You may think of a syllogism as a mathematical structure that can be broken down into
replaceable parts.

Key Premises
A premise is a proposition upon which an argument is based. Any argument yo니 build will be
based on a number of premises. In some cases, you can assume that your reader already agrees
with your premise. In other cases, you cannot. In cases where yo니 cannot assume that yo니r
reader agrees, it is helpful to explain your premise and tell your reader why this premise is valid.

• 전제는 어떤 주장을 기반으로 하는 생각의 표현이다 어떤 주장이라도 수많은 전제들이 바탕이 될 것이다. 만약

An unstated premise is, "It is desirable that Korea be competitive in the global
marketplace."

Q 표출되지않은전제
If yo니 are writing for a general Korean a니dience, you can assume that yo니r reader agrees that it
is desirable that Korea be competitive in the global marketplace. However, you cannot assume
that your reader agrees that science is the most important factor of economic success.
Why?

You can assume that your reader wants Korea to be "competitive in the global marketplace"
because there is a broad consensus among Korean citizens that economic s니ccess is desirable.
Very few Korean people would say they want Korea to fail economically. The vast majority of
people believe that if the economy is healthy, everyone benefits, so this is a shared assumption.

You cannot assume your reader agrees that "science is the most important factor in economic
success" because there is not a broad consensus on this point. This is a controversial claim. Som은
of your readers probably agree, but some probably disagree. Some may think humanities are
more important than science. Some may think b니siness and economics are more important.
Some probably are not sure or have not tho니ght about it. So yo니 shoud not go forward with
your argument until you have explained why you think science is so important. In other words,
you must explain why this premise is valid.

How can you tell when a premise is shared by your audience and when it is not? There is no easy
answer to this question. Yo니 must think abo니t your audience and, if necessary, do some
research.

Of course everyone knows that Politicians are unethical, and I'm s니re that you are as

You can write:

Many Politicians are unethical; the following data demonstrate that this claim is true.

Then present supportive data.

You can also begin your essay:

The distinction here is this: It is accurate to acknowledge that many people think politicians are
unethical. However, others respect politicians and hold them in high regard, so you cannot
assume that yo니r reader agrees with a premise that disparages politicians.

What if yo니r audience is not a general a니dience? You will have to use your judgment to
determine what you can and cannot assume.

If you are writing for a motorcycle magazine, yo니 can assume that your reader likes motorcycles
and is interested in them.

If yo니 are writing for a general international audience, you cannot assume that your reader
wants Korea to be a trendsetter in global culture. However, you can assume this if you are
writing for a Korean a니dience.

Whoever your audience is, consider them carefully and ask yo니rself, "What can I reasonably
ass니me about my readers? What are my premises? Can I assume my readers will accept th은se
premises or do they need to be explained and/or supported?" If you go over your essays with
this in mind, good judgment and common sense should tell you when and how to support your
premises effectively.

Context
Put your subject, the이s, and overall argument in context. This will help your reader to
understand your ideas. A context is the set of facts or circumstances that surrounds a situation
or event. The subject of your essay is connected to many facts and circumstances. Knowing
abo니t them will help your reader to 니nderstand yo니r essay, and will broaden his perspective on
the subject. So think abo니t your s니bject and ask yourself, “Which facts and circumstances
should I explain to my reader so he can better understand my subject?” Try to think of the most
essential “key information” that will enhance your reader’s

니nderstanding.

The information yo니

offer may be historical, philosophical, cultural, economic, psychological, scientific or it may draw
on some other relevant area of knowledge.

Type A thinking is rooted in an ancient religious tradition that teaches the world is a

Reasons

As discussed in the previous chapter, these sections lay out the reasons why you believe your

Previously we used three reasons as a standard number. However, it is not necessary to give

may decide give only a single reason:

In this case, the 외ngle reason is so compelling that there is no need to add other reasons.

On the other hand, in an advanced essay, there may be more than three valid reasons. You are
fr은e to lay out as many reasons as you like. Remember, the idea here is to persuade the reader. If
you believe more reasons will help persuade the reader, then present more reasons. However, in
many cases more reasons will just conf니se the reader or wear him out. Use yo니r judgment to
determine what are your very best reasons and how many of them to use.

• 논제에 대한 이유를 제시할 때, 항상 3가지 이유를 제시해야 하는 것은 아니다. 위의 예제에서처럼 한가지 이유

대한 판단을 잘 해야 한다.

引ZeasiUility

Feasibility is the quality of being doable. Something that is feasible is something that can
actually be done. To 니nderstand how feasibility affects an essay, consider this sample thesis:

O Example A: 실현 불가능한논제

We should send a manned spacecraft into the center of the sun to collect scientific
data and bring it back to earth.

Q 우리는 승무원이 탄 우주선을 태양의 중심으로 보내서 과학적인 정보를 모아 자구로 가지고 오도록 해야만 한다.
To build an essay based on this thesis, you can introduce the subject, 이early state your thesis,
explain why ycnur proposal is extrem이y important, put it in historic, philosophical, and(:니It니ral
context, and give a series of reasons to support your thesis with relevant examples and statistics.
Yet this would all be a waste of energy; ycnur argument would not be accepted by any
reasonable reader and your essay would fail to persuade.

Why?

Beca니se it is not feasible. You are s니ggesting something that cannot be done. No marymade
spacecraft and certainly no human being could survive a flight through the s니n, and we have no
technologies or methods that might make this possible in the foreseeable future.

An essay with an unfeasible thesis cannot be persuasive. Therefore, you should always consider
the feasibility of your thesis. Some thesis statements are

니tterly

unfeasible as in the example

above. On the other hand, some thesis statements do not call for any action, so feasibility is not
an issue.

O Exam이e B: 실현 가능성을논할수없는논제

Classical music is better than contemporary music.

Q 고전 음악이 현대 음악보다 더 낫다.

The Writing Process - Beyond Fundamentals

In this case, the thesis does not call for any action. It is just a matter of opinion; there is nothing
to be done, so feasibility is not an issue. Feasibility only applies when the thesis calls for action.

Among the이s statements that call for action, some are self-evidently feasible, and therefore
there is no need to discuss feasibility.

O Exam이e C: 그자체가실현 가능한논제

Q 친절은 우리의 삶의 질을 향상시킨다. 따라서 항상 주변 사람들에게 친절하려고 노력해야 한다.

It is not necessary to convince the reader that this is feasible; clearly, anyone can be polite if he
chooses to be. You will still need to persuade yo니r reader that your thesis is true, but you will not
need to convince him that it is feasible.

■ 예제 A처럼 실현 불가능한 논제는 설득적일 수 없다. 따라서 항상 논제의 실현 가능성을 고려해야 한다.
• 실현 가능성은 행동을 수반해야 한다. 예제 브처럼 의견을 말하는 논제는 실현 가능성에 대한 거론이 아니다.
• 예제 C는 논제에 대해서는 설득이 필요하지만 실현 가능성이 있는지에 대해서는 ‘친절' 하고자 원한다면 누구나
할 수 있는 것으로 굳이 그 내용에 대해 실현 가능성을 확신시킬 필요는 없다.

Most thesis statements are neither absolutely feasible nor absolutely unfeasible. Their feasibility
is open to question. Where the feasibility of a thesis is debatable, it is advisable to explain to the
reader why you believe it is feasible.

easy to convince the reader that this is a worthy goal; how은ver, many readers would think:

Jrgency

A reader may be convinced by all of your points but still may think:

Yes, I agree with this argument, but I don't have to worry about this now; I'll deal with
it later.

It is a natural tendency for humans to put things off. So where appropriate, it is helpful to arg니e
for the urgency of your thesis. For example:

O Exam 이 e A:

Because violence in films leads to violence in real life, we must curb violence in films.

In addition to your other points, you may want to tell the reader why it is important to deal with
this issue now.

O Example B:

Crimes are taking place every day because of the influence of violent films, and these
crimes are becoming more freq니ent and more deadly. If we do not take steps to stop
these violent films now, many more people will be harmed, and some will die. We
cannot afford to wait any longer to solve this urgent problem.

against black people in America. He established 니rgency this way:

your twenty million Negro brothers smothering in an airtight cage of poverty in the

[April 16,1963]

Con

이

usion

The con이usion to an advanced essay is similar to the concl니sion of a basic essay. You will recap
yo니r argument and restate your thesis, 니sually in a slightly different form. The con이usion may
be short and to the point like a basic essay, or it may be much longer, possibly several
paragraphs of wrapping 니p your various points and tying them together. It is still best to avoid
raising entirely new issues or areas of discussion; the idea generally is to drive home the points
you have already made and leave the reader with a final sense that your thesis is tnje, important,
and valuable.

This model for an advanced persuasive essay should be helpful to you as you learn to write
complex essays. It is not important to follow this model step-by-step; it is important to learn
each of the components in this model -compelling need, counterarguments, feasibility, etc. -so
that yo니 can 니se them in any situation in which they would help to strengthen your argument
and make it more persuasive.

Being a good writer means learning to make good judgments about how best to present yo니r
ideas. To begin with, following a model may be helpful, but in the end, you will want to learn to
follow the flow of your own ideas to build arguments that follow your natural train of tho니ght.

The Advanced Narrative Essay
4'

In the last chapter yo니 learned to write narrative essays by telling abo니t the events in
chronological order, s은tting and resetting the scene as your story moves forward, and
describing the essential action so that your reader can f니lly understand what took place. In this
chapter yo니 will learn to enhance your narratives by creating detailed descriptions and lively
metaphors and similes.

Marc Handler

Ordering the Events
In the last chapter, you learned to prepare your narrative by listing the incidents in chronological
order. It is advisable to prepare an advanced narrative essay the same way.

O Example A:

1.1 left my house.
2.1 drove my car.
3. My car broke down (stopped working).
4.1 got out and started walking.
5.1 tripped and fell in a mud p니ddle.

Once you have established these steps for an advanced narrative essay, you may want to tell
them in a different order. For example, your essay might start at the end, with number five, then
go back and tell the story from step one, like this:

O Example B:

There I was sitting in a puddle, covered with mud. How had this happened? It all
started when I left my house that morning...

H 그때 나는 잔흙을 뒤집어 쓴 채로 흙탕물에 앉아 있었다. 어떻게 된 걸까? 이 모든 것은 아침에 집에서 나왔을 때...
In this case, the story starts at the end, with you in the puddle, and then it goes back to explain
how this happened, that is, how you ended up in the mud puddle.

Advanced narratives often rearrange the order of events in various ways. This can make the
narrative more interesting. Flashbacks are one common way of doing this. Here's an example of
a flashback from the middle of a narrative about a football game:

In this example, the writer is telling abo니t a football game; then he stops and "flashes back" to
something

that

happened

earlier

that

morning.

When

he

finishes

with

the

flashback,

he

contin니es with the story of the football game. So the narrative is not told in chronological order;
the writer is jumping back and forth in time. The flashback gives new information and insight
that makes the story of the game more meaningful.

Changing the order of events is an option, but it is not necessary. If you prefer to tell yo니r story
in direct chronological order, that is fine. In fact, most fundamental and advanced narratives tell
their stories in chronological order. However, it is good to know how to vary the order of events
so that you can do it when you fe이 it will help to make yo니r story more effective.

Vocabulary Tripped: catching the foot on something that causes one to st니mble or fall
Puddle: a small and shallow amount of liquid gathered on a surface
Flashback: an earlier scene or event from the past inserted into a narrative out of the

chronological order of events
Crawling: moving aro니nd on hands and knees
Attic: a room or the area directly below a pitched roof of a building, usually in a house
Rag: piece of clothing that is worn out and no longer worn
Raggedy: torn, dirty and worn out
Streak: unbroken period of good or bad luck as in wins or losses
Goalie: goalkeeper

Use clear transitions when you move to different time periods.

By flashing back or jumping around in time, you can make yo니r narrative more
interesting, but you also risk confusing yo니r reader. To avoid this problem, when you
transition from one time period to another, be careful to 니se clear transitions so that
yo니r reader can follow yen』without getting lost. For example, the narrative above
uses the transitional phrase "that morning, before the game..." This tells the reader
that the narrative has moved from the football game to an earlier time.

Writing Descriptions

Good descriptions are the so니I of good narratives. A narrative is enjoyable and easy to picture
when the descriptions are vivid. How do you write clear, vivid descriptions?

Use your senses.

Descriptions are based on your five sense오 sight, sound, touch, smell, and taste. So whenever
you are describing something, start with yo니r senses. Suppose you are describing an object. Ask:

What does it look like?
What does it sound like?
What does it feel like?
What does it smell like?
What does it taste like?

Describe it in terms of your senses. Then you may add your emotional responses and your
intellectual analysis. Of course, you do not need to use all five senses every time. If you are
describing food, taste will be very important. If yo니 are describing a new car, taste will probably
not be part of your description. If you are describing music, sound will be most important. But
for most descriptions, 외ght will be the most important factor. Humans are visually oriented and
we identify most things primarily by sight.

• 뛰어난 묘사는 상상을 쉽게 해 주고 이야기에 생명을 불어넣어 주는 역할을 한다
■ 이런 묘사는 보고, 듣고, 만지고, 냄새 맡고, 맛보는 오감을 이용해야 한다. 이중 최고의 묘사는 보고 있는 것과
같은 것이다. 그리고 감정적인 반응과 지적인 분석을 더한다면 더 좋은 묘사가 된다.

Descriptions
Read through the following description and circle the parts that are based on sight.
Mark them with an ST. When you’ve finished identifying sight descriptions, circle

Sample Description:

The Bartons lived in a tall red house with a white picket fence shaded by a
spreading rubber tree that dropped annoying little pods all over the yard. The
kids came there every day to play around the tree, their shrill voices frequently
drowned out by the roar of airplanes overhead; the local airport was only two
miles away. That summer the house was freshly painted, so it was smooth to
the touch. The smoothness of the house contrasted with the rough rubber tree
that chafed the children when they tried to climb it. Green and white Jasmine
bushes snaked up trellises in front of the house, scenting the air. The scent of
jasmine a나racted wasps that buzzed and hummed, scaring the children, who
ran from them in over-dramatic fear. Sometimes during the afternoon, Mrs.
Barton would come out wearing a billowy dress with bright stripes, carrying a
plate of chocolate chip cookies. Fresh out of the oven, the cookies felt soft and
warm in the children’s hands, and the sweet gooey chocolate literally melted
onto their tongues. Somehow, they tasted like summer. The children ate as
many as they could stuff into their mouths.

Descriptions
This is a particularly valuable exercise for developing your writing skills. The more
you do this exercise, the stronger your descriptive skills will become.

Describing a Person:

Find a comfortable place. Have writing tools at hand (a pen and paper or
computer with word processing program). Lie down or sit comfortably. Close
your eyes. Just breathe for a while and listen to your breathing. Then think of a
person. It could be someone from your past or someone you know now. Think of
them in a particular place at a particular time.
Samples:

My grandmother when I visited her house during the New Year holiday.
My first boyfriend when he arrived at my house on our first date.
My father when he washed the car.
That strange looking man I saw in the park.
My friend when I visited her in the hospital.
Once you have chosen the person, use each of your senses to remember the person.
Follow the steps of the exercise below. (It may be helpful to have someone read the
exercise to you softly while your eyes are closed, or you can read the exercise into a

to picture the person.)

This kind of writing is called a "character description."

You can use this same exercise to describe places and objects.

You can also use this exercise to describe something that you are looking at NOW. Describe the
room that you are in. Describe the person who is with yo니 now. Describe your car or motorbike
while you are looking at it. Use the same approach: sight, sound, touch, taste, smell, emotions,
analysis. In time, this approach will become second nature and you will be able to describe
anything naturally and effectively.

Note that in each case you will want to consider all five senses. However, you will not always
write about all five senses. In some cases, you will see that some senses do not apply to this
particular subject. For example, in some cases you will find there is no important taste or smell
associated with your subject. In that case, don't write about taste or smell; j니st write about the
senses that seem important.

The more you do this exercise, the more you will learn to use your senses, emotions, and
intellect to explore your subjects, and the stronger your descriptions and yo니r narrative writing
will become.

Metaphors
Another great tool for describing people or things is the metaphor. Typically, a metaphor is a
very short, very effective way to communicate your impressions. When you use metaphors, you
take a word or phrase that ordinarily describes one thing, and you use it to describe something
else. The idea is to find a metaphor that creates a dear, striking description of the s니bject.

• 사람이나 사물에 대한 또 다른 묘사 방법으로 은유metaphor） 가 있다.

O Example A: He is a dynamo.

H 그는발전기다.
This phrase describes a person as an engine. It implies that he is energetic and forcef니I like an
engine.

O Example B: She is a bookworm.

Q 그녀는 책벌레다.
This phrase describes a person as a kind of insect. It implies that she is very involved in reading.

O Example C: That car is a lemon.

H 저 차는레몬이다.
This phrase describes a car as a kind of fruit. Since lemons taste sour, it implies that the car is bad.

O Example D: That campus is a maze.

H 저캠퍼스는하나의미로다.

Metaphor and Culture

Metaphors are often culturally based. For example, if yo니 write:

"Ki Hoon really struck out with that girl."

This implies that Ki Hoon was trying to get to know a girl, but she rejected him.

The metaphor comes from baseball. To "get" this metaphor, the reader has to understand
something about baseball: Striking out is bad; it means the batter cannot get on a base so he
cannot score a n』n for his team. Thus, in any country where baseball is a common sport, such as
Korea, the USA, Japan, Cuba, or The Dominican Republic, baseball metaphors can be effective.
However, if you are writing for an audience in Europe, Africa, or Southern Asia, most people do
not know how to play baseball, so this kind of metaphor would not be effective.

Similarly, metaphors based on Ch'ajon-nori would only work in Korea, but a metaphor based on
winds or rains in Korea would probably work anywhere in the world, since all places experience
wind and rain.

Suppose you want to describe someone who gets angry easily and often. A writer in Mexico
might call s니ch a person "a jalapeno" (chili pepper). B니t this would only work in co니ntries that
eat this kind of chili pepper. In Korea, you might compare them to jjam bbong; again, this
comparison would only work in Korea. On the other hand, if you call such a person "a hothead,"
this metaphor would probably work in most cultures.

So when you write metaphors, be awar은 of their cultural basis. Make sure the metaphor will
work for yo니r target reader.

• 은유법을 사용할 때에는 그 나라의 문화에 맞추어 적용해야 한다. 멕시코에서는 "할로피뇨(매운 고추)"를 일컬어
쉽게 자주 화를 내는 사람을 나타내는 말이다. 이것은 매운 고추를 먹는 나라에서는 함께 동감할 수 있는 말이
된다. 이렇게 문화의 배경이 비슷한 나라에서 사용하는 은유법은 읽는 이가 확실히 이해할 수 있다.

문학을 공부하면 세상에 대한 이해와 상상력을 키울 수 있다. 뿐만 아니라 읽고 분석하고, 의미를 이끌어 내고,

In the most typical kind of literary assignment, you will be asked to read a story or a novel and to
write an essay about it. There are two aspects to fulfilling such an assignment, and it is important
to understand the difference between them.

1. You must understand the theme of the work, the main point that the author is trying to
comm니nicate with this piece of literature.

B 당신은 작가가 이 글을 통해 전달하고자 하는 주제에 대해 이해해야만 한다.
2. You must present your own thesis, the main point that you are trying to make abo니t this
piece of literature.

H 당신은 이 글을 통해 주제라 할 수 있는 당신 고유의 논제를 제시해야 한다.
Your thesis and the author's main point are two separate things. In some cases, they may agree;
in some cases they may be opposites. You must understand and treat each one separately.

For example, if you are asked to write about The Taming of the Shrew by William Shakespeare,
you may analyze it like this:

O Shakespeare s Theme:

Women should be passive. They should not be bold or express themselves freely.
(Of course this is only one possible interpretation of Shakespeare's theme, there are
many others)

There is no 이ngle correct format for a literary essay and no single correct way to write one.
However, it may be helpful to have a model for a basic literary essay. This is not the only way to
write a literary essay, and this model will not work for all assignments. When your teacher
assigns you to write a literary essay, read the assignment carefully and follow the instructions.
However, for most literary essay assignments, this model can provide a helpful starting point for
planning and writing your essay.

• 문학적인 에세이 과제를 제출해야 할 경우, 교수가 제공하는 자료와 더불어 아래의 방법이 글을 계획하고 작성
하는데 도움이 될 수 있다.

:: Chart 5 - Model for the 니terary Essay about a Novel, Story, or Dramatic Work

Introduction

Title, Author, Subject (what the story is about)

Summarize the Story

Beginning (main question)
Middle
End (resolution of main question)

Author's Theme(s)

Author's Main Reason(s)
(why he thinks his theme is true)

Examples from Text
(illustrate the author's theme)

Your Thesis

Your Main Points
(why you think your the이s is true)

Example(s) from Your Life
(illustrate your thesis with experiences from your own life)

Conclusion

Summarize Your Main Points
Restate Your Thesis

If you are asked to write an essay about a work of poetry, you can take a similar approach, but
you will need to adapt it depending on the type of poetry you are interpreting. Often you will be
more concerned with the overall "feeling" or "impression" you get from the work, rather than
any "point" or "theme" that it conveys.

• 한편의 시에 관해 에세이를 써야 할 경우, 의미를 파악해 번안하는 것이 필요할 것이다. 주제나 포인트를 지적하
는 것보다는 "느낌"과 "인상"에 대해 더 강조해야 할 것이다.

Let's examine this model in more detail.

Andy Dufrane is imprisoned for a crime he did not commit. He is jumped and

permission to create a library in the prison. This library becomes a sanctuary for the
other prisoners, and Andy becomes a highly respected member of the prison
population. He gets the protection he needs, and his life becomes easier. Then
Andy makes friends with a new prisoner named Tommy. Tommy has information
that could help prove Andy's innocence and get him released from prison.
However, the warden does not want Andy to leave the prison, so he has Tommy
killed. He then punishes Andy, establishing total control
over him. Andy seems to give in, becoming the warden's
submissive assistant. But then he escapes by crawling o니t
through a tunnel he has been patiently digging for years.
Once he is free, Andy anonymou외y supplies the press with
evidence of the warden's crimes. To avoid a scandal, the
warden kills himself. Andy escapes to Mexico where he
lives peacefully. Years later, when Red is released from
prison, he joins Andy in a beautiful Mexican beach town.

There are other types of literary essays as well, essays that analyze poetry, plays, and works of
non-fiction. Each of these requires a somewhat different approach. However, if yo니 modify and
adapt this model, you can use it as a starting point to analyze any kind of literature.

Once you become comfortable writing literary essays, yo니 will not need to follow any model.
The story you are discussing and your own personal writing style will dictate the structure of
yo 니 r essay.

CONCLUSION
In the last two chapters, you have worked with models for Basic Persuasive and Narrative
Essays, Advanced Persuasive and Narrative Essays, and Literary Essays. There are many
other kinds of essays. However, if you master persuasive, narrative, and literary essays,
you will be able to adapt your skills to any kind of essay that you write.
Remember, great writing is not about following a formula or trying to please your teacher.
Great writing is about YOU, thinking deeply about your subject, gaining understanding
and insight, forming ideas and opinions, then communicating your thoughts clearly to the
reader.

Contents
Start by examining your contents. Is your work engaging? Will it capture and hold your reader's
attention? Yo니 might not always have a choice of topics; teachers often assign specific topics in
composition

이asses,

but within the limits imposed by the topic, your goal should be to make

seen hundreds of times before. Be original and reflect yourself and your individuality in yo니r
writing.

특정 주제를 지정해 주는 경우가 많기 때문에 토픽을 항상 자유롭게 선택할 수 있는 것은 아니지만, 토픽이 안고
있는 제약에도 불구하고 가능한 한 재미있게 에세이를 작성해야 한다. 읽는 이들이 수백 번도 더 보았을 진부한

Also, make sure that you remain focused on the topic throughout the paragraph or essay.
Academic writing, unlike conversation, requires that we not stray off topic. While digresskms are
natural in conversation, they are highly distracting in written work, and yo니 don't want to risk
losing your reader's attention by wandering off on an irrelevant tangent. Likewise, make sure
that you haven't accidentally omitted any important details. If yo니 refer to a point in yo니r thesis
and then neglect to develop it in your essay, your reader will be confused and possibly
disappointed. I tell my composition students that their theses are like contracts with their
readers, and they don't want to make promises they can't or don't keep.

and they take up more than one space. Th은y make a lot of noise too. The parking

The a니thor does make his point clear, but he is not very focused. He doesn't really provide

having to get up and come to campus early than on the parking lot itself. This essay is far too
conversational in its tone and development.

Organization
The next step in revising is your organization. At this point, you need to examine yo니r
paragraph(s) and make sure that as individual parts they form a cohesive whole. This is achieved
by making sure that each paragraph develops one main idea and that the main idea is made
이ear

in a topic sentence. The topic sentence needs to have a clear controlling idea, your attitude

or opinion about the topic, and all of the supporting sentences must develop and support the
controlling idea.

H 수정에 있어서 다음 단계는 구설이다. 이 단계에서는 각 문단들을 검사하고, 글의 각 부분들이 전반적으로
일관성 있게 구성되어 있는지 확인해야 한다. 이를 위해서는 각 문단들마다 주제가 있는지, 그리고 이 주제가 토픽
문장에서 명확하게 드러나는지를 확인해야 한다. 토픽 문장에는 명확한 중심 주제와 토픽에 대한 견해나 의견이
있어야 하며, 이렇게 어우러진 문장들은 중심 주제를 전개시키고 뒷받침해야 한다.

Now is also the time to make sure that yo니r paragraph or essay follows a logical order and that
the supporting details are also presented in a logical sequence. If something is unclear to yen』,
the writer, how can you possibly expect it to be clear to your reader? Organization is the key to a
reader-friendly piece of writing.

H 이때에는 또한 문단과 글이 논리적인 순서대로 작성되었는지, 뒷받침하는 세부 사항들이 차례대로 소개되었
는지 확인해야 한다. 작성자가 보기에도 불분명한 것을 읽는 이가 어떻게 명확하게 이해할 수 있겠는가? 읽는 이
에게 읽기 편한 글을 만들려면 무엇보다도 우선 글의 구성이 잘되어 있어야 한다.

As you work on improving the quality of your writing, it is important to make it as coherent as

logically from one into the next within a paragraph, and paragraphs flow smoothly and logically
in an essay.

This step is editing. If you are writing a process essay and are working on m니tiple drafts, then
you would edit the second draft after you have taken care of the revision of yo니r contents,
organization, and coherence in the first draft. If you don't have the luxury of time, you still need
to get into the habit of reading your work carefully and correcting any problems you find before
you turn in your work. All too often students rush out of class as soon as they finish their in-class
essays, taking advantage of being able to leave a few minutes earlier than usual instead of
spending those extra minutes editing their writing. The mistakes they miss can add up quickly
and mean a C instead of the B they might have gotten had they been more cautious and
prudent.

Act니al learning is the res니t of time and practice and caref니 attention paid to details. But it is
possible. Many people have successfully learned new languages and become as comfortable
expressing themselves in their second languages as they are in their first. It is necessary for the
learner to realize that he is in control and that he determines how m니ch and how well he learns.
A successful learner doesn't expect someone else to do his work for him. He knows that he has
to be constantly alert, paying attention to the language around him at all times. He needs to
connect what he sees and hears and reads to what he already knows. It is by making these
connections, understanding the relationships between words and ideas, that s니ccess is possible.

H 실제로 어떤 것을 배우기 위해서는 상세한 내용까지 시간과 공을 들여야 한다. 많은 사람들이 성공적으로 새
로운 언어를 배워 왔으며 모국어처럼 구사할 수 있게 되었다. 학생들은 얼마나 많은 내용을 얼마나 잘 배울 것인
가를 자신이 통제하고 결정할 수 있다는 사실을 깨달아야 한다. 성공적인 학생은 남이 자신의 일을 대신해 줄 거
라고 기대하지 않는다. 항상 긴장하고 언제나 주변에서 사용하는 언어에 주의를 기울여야 한다는 사실을 알고 있
다. 자신이 보고 듣고 읽은 내용들을 이미 알고 있는 내용과 연결시킬 필요가 있다. 말과 생각 사이의 연관 관계를
이해하고 그것들을 연결할 수 있게 되면 외국어 배우기에 성공할 수 있을 것이다.

The following samples are authentic

here range from level one, which
begins with basic sentence-level
writing, through levels four and five,
university transfer credit courses which focus on
writing extended essays and research papers. Level five is the last ESL
course prior to enrollment in Freshman Composition, where students
are mainstreamed with native English speakers.
B 다음에 나오는 예제들은 2년제 대학의 ESL 과정 학생들이 작성한 에세이이다 이 대학에는 ESL
과정을 수강하는 학생들이 많으며, ESL 프로그램도 잘 완비되어 있어서 거의 모든 기법을 배울 수 있
다. 숙련도에 따라 레벨 1부터 레벨 5까지 있다. 레벨 1에서는 간단한 문장을 작성하는 것부터 배우며,
레벨 4나 5에서는 길이가 긴 에세이나 연구보고서 작성과 같은 것을 배운다. 레벨 4나 5는 대학간 학
점 상호 인정제의 적용을 받는다. 레벨 5는 ESL의 최고 과정이며, 대부분의 학생들이 네이티브 스피
커이며 대학 신입생 작문에 등록하기 직전 과정이다.

While the TOEFL is not required for admission to many two-year colleges, including the
one represented here, a score of 450 is usually considered the minimum by those that do
use it in their assessment and placement of international students. The students whose
essays are assembled here were placed into the courses based on multiple measures,
criteria which consider a student's first language, years of education completed in the
United States, and years of English study, among other factors.

These essays were scored holistically by ESL teachers according to a rubric very similar to

only 30 minutes to write and cannot use dictionaries, our students had approximately 45
minutes to write and were able to use dictionaries.
Q 학생들이 제출한 에세이들은 ETS에서 TWE 에세이용으로 개발한 것과 매우 유사한 항목에 따라 ESL 교수
들이 전체적으로 채점을 한다. 그러나 TWE에서는 작성 시간이 30분이며 사전을 사용할 수 없지만, 여기의 경우
에는 으I 45분 정도가 주어지며 사전을 사용할 수 있다.

The essays were each read by two readers, instructors currently teaching the level being
evaluated, who assigned them letter grades based on the criteria developed by the ESL
program. If there was disagreement, for example, one professor passing and another
professor failing an essay, it was then read by a third reader. The readers keep their own
records of their grades and do not know what grades the others are assigning until the class
set has been read. At that point, the grades are tallied and discrepant papers are read for a
third time. The grades that you see here reflect the final scores assigned to the essays.
H 각 에세이들은 해당 레벨을 현재 가르치고 있는 2명의 교수가 채점하며, ESL 프로그램에서 개발한 기준에 따
라 A, B, C 식으로 등급을 매긴다. 두 채점관 간에 이견이 있는 경우 （예를 들어 한 명은 '통과­ 를, 다른 한 명은 '낙
제'를 준 경우） 에는 다른 세 번째 교수가 읽어본다. 채점관들은 독립적으로 채점을 하며, 다른 채점관이 매긴 점수
는 과정 배치가 발표될 때까지 알지 못한다. 이때 각 채점관들이 매긴 점수가 일치하는지 검사를 하게 되며, 일치하
지 않는 에세이는 세 번째 채점을 하게 된다. 이 책에 나오는 에세이들에 할당된 점수들은 모두 최종 점수이다.

Q Level One Essay #2:
When I was Lost

This story happened on my eighteen age. On that year I was studying on a
nursing school in Shanghai. One day I went back home from school. I lost my way.
I arrived in a strange place. Before I never went there. I didn't know which bus
sho니Id I took. I was very upset because the day was getting dark. I saw an old
man on the bus stop. I went up to ask for helping. I told him my direction that I
wanted to go. The old man was very kind. He told me I should took the No. 5 bus
first, then changed the No. 55 bus. No.5 bus stop was across from this street. I
should got three stops then got down and changed No. 55 b니s. They were on the
same stop. I thanked him for his help. According to his directions, I went back
home.

vocabulary;

■ Generally correct use of negative statements;

Sample Essays from Level Two Students

Essay Guidelines:
A passing essay has at least one paragraph of at least 150-200 words and a controlling idea. It
has generally correct and understandable sentence structure, with som은 use of coordination
and subordination (and, soz but, because, when). The essay shows control of these grammar
points:

H 에세이작성 지침.: 이 단계를 통과하려면 최소 150〜200 단어, 최소 한 문단으로 구성된 중심 주제가 있는

에세이를 작성해야 한다. 대등절이나 종속절(and, so, but, because, when)을 사용해서 문장 구조가 어느 정도
정확하고 이해 가능해야 한다. 이 에세이에서는 다음과 같은 문법적인 사항을 다루어야 한다.

• Parts of speech, especially in subject and verb positions;
• Simple present, past, and future tenses and present and past progressive
tenses;
• Modal a니xiliaries: can, should, may, might, could, have to, m니st;
• Count / noncount nouns, pronouns, articles, and quantifiers;
• Consistent errors in these areas are grounds for failure.

• 품사(品詞), 그 중에서도 특히 주어와 동사의 위치
• 간단한 현재, 과거, 미래 시제, 현재 진행형, 과거 진행형
• 법 조동사: can, should, may, might, could, have to, must
• 가산/불가산 명사, 대명사, 관사, 수량 형용사
■ 위에 언급한 내용들을 계속해서 틀릴 경우 과정을 통과하지 못한다.

Essay Guidelines:
A pas외ng essay has at least one paragraph of at least 200-250 words, with a 이ear controlling
idea and a clear beginning, middle, and end. It has generally correct and understandable simple
and compound sentence structure, with some use of transition signals and subordination,
especially in time cla니ses. The essay should show control of these grammar points:

Q 에세이 작성 지침: 이 단계를 통과하려면 최소 200〜250 단어, 최소 한 문단으로 구성된 중심 주제가 있는 에
세이를 작성해야 한다. 에세이에는 서론, 본론, 결론이 명확하게 있어야 한다. 연결어와 종속절（특히 시간절）을 사용
한 단문이나 중문으로서, 문장 구조가 어느 정도 정확하고 이해 가능해야 한다. 이 에세이에서는 다음과 같은 문법적
인 사항을 다루어야 한다.

• Simple and progressive tenses;
• Modal a니xiliaries in present and past forms;
. Appropriate parts of speech;
• The following are introduced in level three and may not be as well controlled
as the points listed above: present perfect, past perfect, and real conditionals;
active / passive voice.

’ 단순 시제나 진행형
■

법 조동사: 현재형, 과거형

• 다음은 레벨 3에서 처음 소개되었는데, 이전에 배웠던 것들에 비해 사용하기 힘들 것이다: 현재 완료, 과거 완료,
조건문, 수동태/능동태

Note: Students should not be penalized or rewarded for the number of paragraphs they wrote.
Emphasis should be placed on cohesiveness and length （ 200-250 words） .

Essay Guidelines:
A passing essay has at least 250-300 words and 니ses paragraph organization with a controlling
idea and a clear beginning, middle, and end. It has appropriate use of transition signals and
some variety in vocabulary. The essay has generally correct and understandable simple,
compound, and complex sentences. The essay shows control of these grammar points:

H 에세이 직•성 지침: 이 단계를 통과하려면 최소 250〜300 단어로 구성된 중심 주제가 있는 에세이를 작성해
야 한다. 에세이에는 서론, 본론, 결론이 명확하게 있어야 한다. 연결어와 다양한 단어들을 충분히 사용해야 한다.
단문이나 중문, 복문이 고루 포함되어 있고, 문장 구조가 정확하고 이해 가능해야 한다. 이 에세이에서는 다음과
같은 문법적인 사항을 다루어야 한다.

■ Control of subordinate clauses: adjective, noun, and adverb clauses and their
reduced forms;
■ Control of all verb tenses, in이uding perfect and hypothetical conditionals;
• Appropriate use of all parts of speech;
■ Consistent errors in these areas are gro니nds for fail니re.

• 형용사절, 명사절, 부사절과 그 축약형들을 담은 종속절을 활용한다.

What is Important in 니fe
People judge other people from their position and what they have. Especially

the most important thing to be better person is not to have nice things and good

always feel that something is missing. They don't know what they are missing, but

INTRODUCTION

Like all languages, English is constantly evolving. Over time, many rules have fallen by
the way side, and usages that were once considered incorrect are now widely accepted,
even in formal academic writing. For instance, just a few decades ago, most English
teachers taught their students never to split infinitives. In other words, it was considered
grammatically incorrect to place a modifier between the word to and the verb that
followed it in an infinitive. Probably the most famous split infinitive in the English
language was used in the opening of the television series Star Trek: “to boldly go where no
man has gone before.” Some traditionalists still believe that infinitives should not be split,
but English-speakers have been splitting infinitives for centuries, and most authorities now
agree that there’s nothing wrong with the practice. The Oxford English Dictionary,
probably the most respected authority on usage rules, recently declared that split
infinitives are acceptable in standard English.
H 다른 언어와 마찬가지로 영어도 끊임 없이 진화하고 있다. 시간이 지남에 따라 많은 규칙들이 사라지고, 예전

에는 잘못된 것으로 간주되던 용법들이 자금은 널리（심지어는 정식 학교 작문 시간에서도） 받아들여지기도 한다. 예
를 들어 수십 년 전만 하더라도 대부분의 영어 교수들은 학생들에게 절대 부정사를 분리시키지 말라고 가르쳤다.
즉, to 부정사에서 to와 동사 사이에 수식어구를 넣는 것이 문법적으로 틀린 것으로 간주되었다. 아마도 분리 부정
사 중에서 가장 유명한 것은 W 시리즈 •스타트랙’의 시작 부분에 나오는 “to tx）l 에 y go where no man has
gone before.”일 것이다. 전통주의자들은 아직도 부정사를 분리 사용해서는 절대로 안된다고 믿고 있지만, 지난 수

백 년간 분리 부정사를 사용해 왔으며 지금은 대부분의 권위자들도 실용성을 가진 것에 잘못된 것이 아니라고 인정
하고 있다. 최근에는 용법에 있어서 아마도 최고의 권위를 가진 옥스포드 사전에서도 분리 부정사를 표준 영어로
인정하고 있다.

Likewise, the meanings of words change over time. Many words in English have different
meanings today than they did one or two hundred years ago. Definitions change when
people begin to use words in new ways, often to the dismay of English teachers and other
authorities. But though self-appointed guardians of a language may do all they can to stop
people from “misusing” words, it’s often a losing battle: The evolution of any language
is an unstoppable process.

The New Oxford Dictionary of English. Ed. Judy Pearsall. Oxford, UK: Oxford UP, 1998.

교수나 작가들에게는 당황스럽겠지만, 사람들이 단어를 새로운 방법으로 사용하면 단어의 뜻이 바뀐다. 자칭 ‘언어
의 수호자’들이 이러한 단어의 '오용,을 고쳐보려고 시도해 보기도 하지만 대개의 경우 뜻을 이루지 못한다. 언어의
진화는 막을 수가 없다.

Common Word Usage Mistakes

NOTE: Consensus is often misspelled concensus. Since the

훈련이 되었다 즉, 내가 꿈꾸는 일자리를 잡았을 때 그에 대한

떻게 비즈니스에 도움이 돠는자를 이해하게 된다 도넛 가게에서

준비가 되어 있을 것이란 소리다. 입사 면접을 위해서 새로운 면

일한 것이 이런 일들을 직접 체험할 수 있도록 해 주었다. 고객

접관을 만나더라도 두렵지 않을 것이다. 면접관과 얘기하는 것은

관계나 직원 관계 등에 대한 구체적인 정보를 더 많이 얻을수록,

도넛 가게에서 고객과 얘기하는 것과 다를 것이 없기 때문이다.

미래에 원하는 목표에 도달하기 위한 준비가 더 잘 될 것이다.

출납원으로 일한 덕분에 새로운 사람들과 대화를 하더라도 자신
만만하며, 예상치 못한 질문도 더 쉽게 대처할 수 있다. 그 동안

결론적으로, Diamond Donuts에서의 개인적인 경험은, 더 큰 직

여러 번 면접을 볼 기회가 있었다. 비록 찾던 일자리를 잡자는 못

업 목표를 향해 노력해 나가는 사람들에게 임시 직업이 매우 도

했지만, 면접에 대해서 자신감이 있으며, 어떤 질문을 받더라도

움이 될 수 있다는 사실을 확신하게 해 주었다. 가족에게 기여함

대답할 준비가 되어 있다. 이런 사살들을 통해 나는, 임시 직업은

으로써 책임감 있는 사람이 될 수 있게 만들고, 게다가 미래에 가

자기 자산을 활력 있고 현명하게 유지시키며, 새로운 일자리나

지게 될 기회에 대해 만반의 준비를 할 수 있고、특정한 직업의

면접에 대해 민첩하게 준비하는 효과적인 방법이라는 것을 확신

가술을 가르치며, 특히 고객과 직원과의 유대관계에 대해 이해할

하게 되었다.

수 있게 해 준다. 우리가 꿈꾸는 직업을 갖기까지 기다리는 동안

임시 직업의 세 번째 이점은 다른 일자리를 잡았을 때 유용하게

임시직은 매우 유익할 수 있다.

써먹을 수 있는 새로운 구체적인 지삭들, 특히 동료 직원들과 함
께 한 팀에서 일하는 법을 배울 수 있다는 점이다.

［이 통계차들은 모두 임의의 샘플이다

임시 직업은 팀원들의 가분을 이해하는 방법을 가르쳐 주기도 하
며, 또한 팀을 운영하는 방법을 배울 기회를 제공해 주기도 한다.
이것은 회사에서 경력을 쌓고자 계획하고 있는 사람에겐 도움이
된다.

나의 경우에는 Diamond Donuts에서 일한 경험이 고객과 직접
일할 때 특히 도움이 많이 되었다. 나중에 내가 관리직에 있을 때

===== PAGE 567 =====
6장과 7장에 수록된 에세이의 한글 번역본 입니다.

살인범 Ted Bundy는 음란 외설 프로그램 때문에 28명의 여자
를 죽이게 되었다고 주장하였다. 다른 범죄자들도 락 음악이나
할리우드 영화 때문에 범죄를 저지르게 되었다고 주장해 왔다
그러나 다른 수백만 명들도 같은 음악을 듣거나 같은 영화를 보
았지만 범좌를 저지르자는 않았다. 이런 것들은 분명히 단지 변
명에 불과 하다. 자신이 저지른 범죄에 대해 비난을 모면해 보려
는 약은 수법일 뿐이다. 공공 정책은 이러한 범죄자들의 그럴듯
한 주장에 귀를 기울여서는 안 된다
।
।

।
।
그렇다면 공공 정책은 무엇을 기반으로 해야 하는가? 간단한 이
성적인 가준, 즉 실제 위험으로부터 공공의 안전을 보장할 수 있
는 한도 내에서 가능한 한 최대의 자유를 기반으로 해야 한다. 지
금 바로 이러한 종류의 가준을 수용하지 않는다면, 해가 갈수록
더 심한 방법으로 언론 통제를 계속한다면, 우리는 곧 표현의 자
유 중 근본적인 부분을 잃게 될 것이며, 그 때에는 되돌리기에는
너무 늦을 것이다. 따라서 정부나 검열 위원회의 간섭, 언론 매체
에서의 폭력 묘사에 대한 강한 통제를 중단해야 한다. 대신 이제
더 나은 （구속하거나 재갈을 물리자는 않지만 자제할 줄 아는） 언
론 매체를 만들어 보자
